# Sourcing screen B: seam-free Obsidian plugins for the seeded `.obsidian/` folder — evidence

- **Date:** 2026-10-03
- **Method:** desk read per candidate family at a pin, then an adversarial verifier per family defaulting to refuted; agents returned per-must results and exit lines only, no verdicts; live legs pending (at 2026-10-03; superseded: the live legs ran on 2026-10-04, §6.5). A supplementary run on 2026-10-03 screened the six floor-vault plugins the first run had not screened (§2.5, §3.5, §4.6) and read the floor vault's configuration (§8), by the same method. On 2026-10-03 a normalisation pass re-judged B3 and B4 for every row under the operator's ruled standards (§2A "Standards as applied"), desk then adversarial verifier; cells it changed carry ‡. A second normalisation pass on 2026-10-04 re-judged B3 for every row under the operator's "judged on what it does by itself" principle (§2A "Second ruling"), desk then adversarial verifier; cells it changed carry §. Live legs ran on 2026-10-04 (Zotero 10.0.5 test instance with snapshot/restore, running Better BibTeX 9.0.68, the versions it ran from the evening of 2026-10-03 after an upgrade from Zotero 10.0.2 with BBT 9.0.64; Obsidian 1.13.7 scratch vault driven through the Obsidian CLI; read-only BBT request replays against production Zotero 10.0.5 with BBT 9.0.63); results carry ◊. A check on 2026-10-04, after the run, re-read one live result against primary sources, and an adversarial verifier re-checked it; cells it changed carry ¤ (§4.5, §7.42). Paths under `/tmp/lane23-live/` are session-local raw logs of the run, archived outside the repository and not retained in it; `/tmp/nwc-check/` held the check's reproduction script, also session-local and not in that archive (§7.42 records its method). A third normalisation on 2026-10-04 re-judged B3 under the operator's trigger-neutral, right-by-construction standard (§2A "Third ruling"), desk then adversarial verifier; cells it changed carry ∆. A fourth reading on 2026-10-04 re-derived B3 under the operator's "beyond editing the open note" ruling (§2A "Fourth ruling"), desk then adversarial verifier; cells it changed carry ◇. On 2026-10-04 a classification pass re-judged B2 against the operator's five workflows, applied three settled readings to B3, and sorted every row as SEED, RECOMMEND, CONDITIONAL or EXCLUDE under "include anything that aids a workflow and does no harm" (§2A "Inclusive rule and five workflows"), a classifier then an adversarial verifier per family (`B-core`, `B-top20`, `B-rest`), the verifier's result recorded; must cells whose result it changed carry ⊕, cells whose result held keep their earlier basis, and §9 carries the sorting with the workflows each row aids.
- **Spec:** 2026-10-03-substrate-components-design.md
- **Binds:** nothing — evidence the spec is built from; the spec carries the verdicts.

**Reading guide.** A mark after a result in §3, §4 and §6.5 says which pass set or changed it, and §4 carries a line for each. **†** marks a cell where the family's adversarial verifier refuted the desk worker (§3 preamble); in §8 it marks a cell the verifier corrected. **‡** marks a B3 or B4 cell the 2026-10-03 normalisation pass changed under the operator's first ruling. **§** placed directly after a result marks a B3 cell the 2026-10-04 second normalisation pass changed under the "judged on what it does by itself" ruling; elsewhere § followed by a number is a section reference. **◊** marks a result or fact from the 2026-10-04 live run (§6.5). It marks an observation, so a cell can read `needs-live◊` when a load attempt was observed but the result turns on a condition the run did not vary (Novel word count B1, §4.5). **¤** marks a cell that a 2026-10-04 post-run check changed; each candidate it touched has a line beginning "¤ 2026-10-04 check" in §4. **∆** marks a B3 cell the 2026-10-04 third normalisation changed under the trigger-neutral ruling (§2A "Third ruling"); each candidate it touched has a line beginning "∆ 2026-10-04 third ruling" in §4. **◇** (a white diamond, distinct from the live run's ◊) marks a B3 cell the 2026-10-04 fourth reading changed under the "beyond editing the open note" ruling (§2A "Fourth ruling"); each such cell has a line beginning "◇ 2026-10-04 fourth ruling" in §4. **⊕** marks a B2 or B3 cell whose result the 2026-10-04 classification pass changed under the operator's inclusive rule and five workflows (§2A "Inclusive rule and five workflows"); it adds no §4 line, and the row's entry in §9 gives the basis. A re-judged B2 cell names the workflows it rests on by number, (1) to (5). A later pass keeps the marks already on a cell. The standards every must was judged under are in §2A "Standards as applied": the 2026-10-03 rulings, and the 2026-10-04 Second ruling, which supersedes them for B3. Every result here is a per-must result, not a verdict: this record adds no verdict, tier or ranking, and the spec carries the verdicts.

Seven worker families ran (at the first run, 2026-10-03; superseded: the supplementary run added `B-floor-vault` and `B-floor-config`, §2.5, §8). `B-core-1` and `B-core-2` screened the two halves of Obsidian's core plugin list. `B-top20-enumeration` formed the community candidate set and screened nothing. `B-community-1`, `B-community-2` and `B-community-3` screened the 20 most-downloaded community plugins and the author's vault seed. `B-search-new` searched for candidates outside those sets and screened its finds. Every family except the enumeration had a desk worker and a verifier. This record carries their per-must results, notes and exit lines. It adds no verdict, tier or ranking. The relayed user request for this run was "as minimum"; it sets no scope beyond the computed task.

## 1. Question and musts

**Question.** Which core plugins (enabled or disabled, and with what settings) and which community plugins does research-vault's seeded .obsidian/ folder ship for a new vault?

**Screen facts as given.** No research-vault Obsidian vault exists yet; the vault template is ~/research-vault/research_vault/templates/vault/ (read its AGENTS.md and system/ to see what a vault holds; it ships two .base files under system/bases/). The seeded folder will carry third-party plugin code into a public repository (assembly spec §6.2: a vendored artifact). The config directory is user-overridable; restricted mode is gated outside the vault. Find the current Obsidian desktop version from obsidianmd/obsidian-releases and record it.

The lane's floor is "Notetaking for Historians" — a prose-first, low-machinery workflow; the repository's reading of it is docs/product-landscape/2026-08-24-history-notes-workflow-comparison.md. OKF conformance rules: docs/agents/okf-conformance.md.

**Shared facts as given (measured 2026-10-03).**

- Step map (closed set; a component serves one of these or none): vault-setup, capture, compile, search, scoping-review, long-form, daily-log, publish.
- Machine surfaces of a vault — only research-vault's CLI writes them: literature/, fulltext/, wiki/, log/, system/, and the review queue. README.md "The vault holds the evidence; the CLI performs every mechanical write".
- Identity: the Better BibTeX citation key is the vault's identity for a source; capture (research_vault/capture.py) carries Zotero's tags and extra verbatim into the literature note's snapshot (CONTEXT.md "Snapshot").

**Musts.**

- **B1** Loads on the current Obsidian in a vault research-vault scaffolded. Always needs-live (tracer T5); record manifest minAppVersion and isDesktopOnly as the desk fact.
- **B2** Serves a step in the step map, or the historians' prose-first low-machinery floor. Name which.
- **B3** Writes nothing into the machine surfaces, or can be configured not to — name the exact setting key and value in data.json / app.json / the core plugin's config that achieves it. Auto-writers (auto-commit, on-create templates, auto-formatters, daily-note creation, attachment moves, file renames that rewrite links) are the cases to examine.
- **B4** Leaves the vault OKF-conformant: what it writes into notes stays readable as plain Markdown without the plugin (cite the OKF rule it touches, if any).
- **B5** Licence permits redistributing its released files (main.js, manifest.json, styles.css) inside a public repository. Record the licence at the pin and any bundled third-party licence notices. Core plugins: n/a (they ship with Obsidian; only their settings are seeded).

### 1.1 Facts the workers recorded for every row

**Current Obsidian desktop: 1.13.7.** It is `latestVersion` at `desktop-releases.json:3`, the in-app update channel. The beta channel is at 1.14.4 (`desktop-releases.json:9`) and `minimumVersion` is 1.1.9 (`:2`). GitHub's `latest` release is v1.13.8 (2026-08-21), but its only asset is `Obsidian-1.13.8.apk`, so it is an Android release. Release v1.13.7 (published 2026-08-12) carries the desktop assets: `.exe`, `.dmg`, `.AppImage`, arm64 `.AppImage`, `.deb`, `.tar.gz` and `obsidian-1.13.7.asar.gz`. `B-core-1`, `B-core-2`, `B-community-3`, `B-search-new` and the enumeration checked the asset lists and agree. `B-community-1`'s desk listed both 1.13.7 and 1.13.8 as candidate current versions, and its open question left the choice open; every minAppVersion in that family is at or below 1.13.0, so no B1 desk fact depends on the choice. This machine's local Obsidian folder holds asars for 1.13.6 and 1.13.7 (`B-community-1` verifier).

**`obsidianmd/obsidian-releases` pins.** Three commits were read as HEAD at different times on 2026-10-03, and all three give `latestVersion` 1.13.7:

| Commit                                                                      | Used by                                                                                     |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `71e006ee3ebdbbc34103445b329167a753e37272` (committed 2026-10-03T21:18:55Z) | `B-core-1` desk, `B-top20-enumeration`, `B-search-new` search log                           |
| `e76f2c31b208ef209b44ab0367b14ed50aa95e03`                                  | `B-core-1` verifier, `B-community-1`, `B-community-2`, `B-community-3`, `B-search-new` desk |
| `a39c6958` (2026-10-01)                                                     | `B-core-2` desk                                                                             |

The default branch is `master`, not `main` (enumeration). `community-plugins.json`, `community-plugin-stats.json` and `desktop-releases.json` fetched from `master` were byte-identical (sha256) to the same files at `71e006e` (search log).

**The core-plugin evidence is the 1.13.7 asar.** Both core families read `app.js` inside `obsidian-1.13.7.asar.gz` from release v1.13.7. Its sha256 is `69253e39aa0b980e3cf96e9e8a8a4bed6b6481ef7021cd762f67872662d8d25a` (`B-core-2`), and its base64 sha256 equals the hash at `desktop-releases.json:5` (`B-core-1`). The asar's `package.json:5` declares the app UNLICENSED and private. `app.js` is one minified line, so cites read `app.js:1@N`.

**Two offset conventions, both kept as given.** `B-core-1` cites `app.js:1@N` as a 0-based **character** (code-point) offset into the UTF-8-decoded file (3,871,377 characters). Its desk text called these byte offsets; its verifier found that byte 3819978 lands in unrelated startup code while character 3819978 is the loadPlugin chain, and corrected the wording without renumbering. `B-core-2` cites `app.js:1@byte N` as a **byte** offset from `grep -b` (the file is 3,876,459 bytes). The same code therefore carries two numbers. For example, the loadPlugin chain is `@3819978` in `B-core-1` and `@byte 3825058` in `B-core-2`, and `editor-status` is `@3365315` and `@byte 3370305`. The cites do not conflict; no cite in this record was renumbered.

**Help pages.** Obsidian's help docs were read at `obsidianmd/obsidian-help@9cf8c2913e56830e75c13f33ba198d7e70b6d9ef`. That repository has no LICENSE file and an empty GitHub licence field, so the workers paraphrased it and quoted nothing. The historians' starter vault (`erazlogo/obsidian-history-vault@a3d22d0a`) has no licence file either (GitHub API 404) and is paraphrased.

**research-vault pin.** Repository cites are at `3780fb7b34617f6dd0f99ee9a747e854c2a80232` unless a family named another. OKF cites are to `SPEC.md@ad30107` (Apache-2.0, recorded by the `B-core-1` verifier).

**B3 reading used by the core families.** `B-core-1` stated the reading it applied to every row and asked the spec to confirm or replace it: pass means no automatic or implicit write into a machine surface, or a named key prevents one; an explicit user gesture on a file the user picked (a cell edit, a drag, a click, a restore) is recorded but does not fail, because it is a hand edit that vault `AGENTS.md:28` and `lints.py` already police; fail means an implicit write, or one gesture fanning out into machine surfaces, with no key to prevent it. Other families did not state a rule, and two verifiers found their families' results inconsistent with each other (§7.1). Resolved 2026-10-04 by the second ruling.

## 2. Candidate set and how it was formed

The set has 65 rows: 31 core plugins, the 20 most-downloaded community plugins, 4 further plugins from the author's vault seed, and 10 search finds (at the first run, 2026-10-03; superseded: the supplementary run added 6 floor-vault rows, §2.5, so the record holds 71).

### 2.1 Core plugins (31 rows; families `B-core-1` and `B-core-2`)

The help list at `obsidian-help@9cf8c29 en/Plugins/Core plugins.md:24-83` has exactly 30 entries, split into two halves of 15. Obsidian 1.13.7 registers 31 plugins in the loadPlugin chain (`app.js:1@3819978`, character offset). The one outside the help list is `editor-status`, which is `hiddenFromList` (`@3365315`) and so absent from the settings list and the help site. `B-core-1` screened it as a 16th row, because `saveConfig` writes every registered id into `core-plugins.json` (`@2919660`), so a seeded file needs a value for it.

The 31 ids are: file-explorer, global-search, switcher, graph, backlink, canvas, outgoing-link, tag-pane, footnotes, properties, page-preview, daily-notes, templates, note-composer, command-palette, slash-command, editor-status, bookmarks, markdown-importer, zk-prefixer, random-note, outline, word-count, slides, audio-recorder, workspaces, file-recovery, publish, sync, bases, webviewer (`B-core-2`). Registration is not uniform: `publish`, `sync` and `webviewer` load only behind build flags (`Ab.publish`, `Ab.sync`, and `isDesktopApp && Ab.webViewer`, at the tail of `@3819978`; `B-core-1` verifier). `Ab.publish` and `Ab.sync` are true in 1.13.7 (`app.js:1@byte 1109776`). All 16 `B-core-1` rows load unconditionally.

`B-core-1` screened: audio-recorder, backlink, bases, bookmarks, canvas, command-palette, daily-notes, file-recovery, file-explorer, footnotes, markdown-importer, graph, note-composer, outgoing-link, outline, editor-status. `B-core-2` screened: page-preview, properties, publish, switcher, random-note, global-search, slash-command, slides, sync, tag-pane, templates, zk-prefixer, webviewer, word-count, workspaces. `B-core-2` flagged `editor-status` and the legacy `starred` id to `B-core-1` and did not screen them.

Dropped by the search worker, with reasons:

- All core plugins, the top 20 and the author-vault set (screen exclusion rule; Dataview, Templater and QuickAdd are also historians-named).
- `obsidian-citation-plugin`, Zotero Integration (`obsidian-zotero-desktop-connector`), zotflow, zotero-redisearch-rag, deep-sit, scholar-sidekick, bibtex-scholar, scholar, obsidian-pandoc-reference-list, CiteMe, copy-as-latex: citation, Zotero or bibliography tools belong to screen C.
- `obsidian-tidy-footnotes` (Heppler-named): same need as `obsidian-footnotes`; last release 0.1.2 (2024-10-15) ships no `styles.css`; the screen may swap it in.
- `better-footnote`: listing not reviewed by Obsidian staff; 6,071 downloads; overlaps `obsidian-footnotes`.
- better-word-count (Heppler-named), advanced-word-count, keep-the-rhythm, writing-goals, obsidian-daily-stats: overlap core Word count and `novel-word-count`; goal and streak trackers serve no step.
- typewriter-mode, cm-typewriter-scroll-obsidian, obsidian-fullscreen-plugin, zen, ultra-zen-mode, composition-mode, writing-menu, scroller: overlap `obsidian-focus-mode`.
- `obsidian-linter`: an auto-formatter that serves no step and rewrites notes, the B3 hazard on machine surfaces.
- obsidian-map-view (Heppler- and forum-named), timeline plugins, canvas-roots: no step, outside the floor.
- storyline, inkswell, draft-bench, book-smith, feuillets (forum-named; bundles CSL citations), web-novel-assistant, writing, colophon-writer, radial-timeline, writing-studio, vault-2-book, book-exporter: Longform already covers the long-form step; most have under 45k downloads or unreviewed listings; Feuillets' citation layer belongs to screen C.
- AI and LLM research assistants (ai-research-assistant, logically, katmer-code, reallygood-research, ai-organiser, perplexed, scholarium, consensus-research, research-paper, literature-review-synthesizer, you-and-your-research, ratel-vault, poneglyph, ogstack, researcher): opposite of the low-machinery floor; synthesis is research-vault's compile step.
- Book metadata and highlight importers (obsidian-book-search-plugin, obsidian-kindle-plugin, book-search-plus, global-book-search, kr-book-info-plugin, easy-obookshelf, book-clipper, papers, arxiv-papers, academic-rss-reader): capture belongs to Zotero.
- Daily-log alternates (daily-notes-editor, obsidian-rollover-daily-todos, obsidian-day-planner, journal-review, obligator, diarian, obsidian-daily-named-folder, review-obsidian): Periodic Notes and core Daily notes represent the step; rollover and obligator write into daily notes automatically; day planner is task time-blocking.
- Export alternates (docx-exporter, to-word, export-to-html, academic-export, mobile-pdf-exporter, book-exporter): overlap the two Pandoc exporters; low adoption or unreviewed.
- docxer, word-reader: Word import or preview; capture belongs to Zotero.
- `NicklasVraa/Obsidian-academic-export`: not in `community-plugins.json@71e006e`, no store install path.
- index-cards: 1,121 downloads, unreviewed, own card format; overlaps core Canvas and Note composer.
- LaTeX and maths plugins (obsidian-latex-suite, quick-latex, obsidian-completr, obsidian-tikzjax, latex environments, equation-citator, academic-notes): not the floor, no step.
- Heppler-named others (CustomJS, Enhanced Annotations, Freeform, Micro.publish, Stille, Multi State Checkbox Switcher): scripting or annotation machinery outside the floor, publishing to a third-party microblog, or task-state UI.
- Other regex matches (CJK counters, trading and teaching planners, 3D embeds, WeChat publishers, kanban-for-bases views): off-topic.

### 2.5 Floor vault (github.com/erazlogo/obsidian-history-vault at commit a3d22d0a9ecf5592db40ef3a6a53b356553a4295; 6 rows)

The relayed user request for the supplementary run asked that every plugin in the floor vault's `.obsidian/plugins/` be included in the screening. The floor vault ships 17 community plugins: the 17 folders under `.obsidian/plugins/` map one to one onto the 17 entries of `.obsidian/community-plugins.json:2-18`, all enabled (`a3d22d0a`; both supplementary families). The computed task gave the pin as `a3d22d0a5ecf5592db40ef3a6a53b356553a4295`; that object does not exist (the GitHub commits API answers 422, and `git cat-file` fails in a fresh clone). The floor's `master` HEAD is `a3d22d0a9ecf5592db40ef3a6a53b356553a4295` (2023-05-27), and every floor cite in this record, old and new, is at that commit; the short form `a3d22d0a` matches both strings (§7.25). The floor repository has no licence (GitHub licence field null), so its contents are paraphrased throughout.

Eleven of the 17 were already screened: ten in this record and one in screen C.

- Top-20 rows (§3.2): Templater (`templater-obsidian`), Dataview (`dataview`), Kanban (`obsidian-kanban`), QuickAdd (`quickadd`), Minimal Theme Settings (`obsidian-minimal-settings`).
- Search finds (§3.4): Longform (`longform`), Advanced URI (`obsidian-advanced-uri`), Footnotes (`obsidian-footnotes`), Focus Mode (`obsidian-focus-mode`), Pandoc Plugin (`obsidian-pandoc`).
- Screen C: Zotero Integration (`obsidian-zotero-desktop-connector`), a citation tool routed to screen C by `B-search-new` (§2.4) and screened in `2026-10-03-substrate-components-design-obsidian-zotero-screen-evidence.md` (its §3 Zotero Integration row, pinned at 3.2.1; the floor vendors 2.3.9).

That leaves six, screened here as family `B-floor-vault`: Auto Note Mover (`auto-note-mover`), Better Word Count (`better-word-count`), Typewriter Scroll (`cm-typewriter-scroll-obsidian`), Contextual Typography (`obsidian-contextual-typography`), Hider (`obsidian-hider`) and Tag Wrangler (`tag-wrangler`). `B-search-new` had dropped two of them by overlap (Better Word Count against core Word count and `novel-word-count`; Typewriter Scroll against `obsidian-focus-mode`; §2.4); they are screened here because the user's request names every floor plugin. The other four had no row and no recorded exclusion. With these six the record holds 71 rows. Each row pins the current upstream release, as the earlier rows do; the floor's vendored version is recorded beside it in §4.6, and the version skew across all 17 is §7.26.

`B-floor-vault` resolved ids through `obsidianmd/obsidian-releases@f8d2f512d402e3f14df60f08a4560c59da57f249` (committed 2026-10-04T00:05:26Z), a fourth pin beside §1.1's three; `community-plugins.json` at that pin was byte-identical to `master` when read. All six ids are listed, none is in `community-plugins-removed.json`, and no repository is archived. The family recorded an empty exclusion list. A seventh name, `obsidian-reading-time`, appears only as a pinned command in the floor's `.obsidian/command-palette.json:5`; it is in neither `.obsidian/plugins/` nor `community-plugins.json`, so it is a stale reference outside the named folder and is owed no row (§8.2 Command palette, §7.32). A second supplementary family, `B-floor-config`, read the floor's `.obsidian/` configuration and screened nothing (§8).

## 2A. Standards as applied (2026-10-03; second ruling 2026-10-04)

On 2026-10-03 the operator ruled on the readings this record had left open. A normalisation pass then re-judged B3 and B4 for all 71 rows under those rulings, with a desk worker and then an adversarial verifier. Where the verifier refuted the re-judgement, the verifier's result is the one recorded. In §3, ‡ marks a cell whose result changed from the draft; §4 carries one line per changed cell, and a line for each unchanged cell whose basis or seed keys moved. For B3 and B4, these rulings replace the machine-surface list in §1's shared facts and the `B-core-1` gesture reading in §1.1. Both are kept as the draft's inputs.

**Machine surfaces.** The machine surfaces are exactly the guard's roster, `hooks/pretooluse_guard.py:11-23` (identical at `3780fb7` and HEAD). Pre-lane-2 spec decision 11 makes the guard authoritative: the vault template's roster is derived from the guard's constants by a test (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-17-pre-lane-2-design.md:27`). The roster covers any path whose first component is `literature/`, `log/`, `wiki/` or `fulltext/`; anything under `system/propagations/`; and the root files `log.md`, `inbox/review-queue.md` and `system/bibliography.json`. The rest of `system/` (`system/templates/`, `system/bases/`, `system/glossary.md`) is hand-edited and is not a machine surface. `README.md:7` names `system/` whole among the surfaces only the CLI writes. That wording is broader than the guard, and it is recorded as a README drift, not as the standard. This answers §7.3.

**B3 (seam-free Obsidian plugins; screen C's C1 takes the same reading) (superseded 2026-10-04).** B3 judges only writes into a machine surface that the person does not make by choosing the file:

- background or automatic writes;
- writes on create or on open;
- bulk or vault-wide rewrites, such as a tag rename across files, link updates after a rename or move, or a property rename across the vault;
- remote or server writes, such as REST, URI, sync or a git pull;
- files the plugin creates or moves on its own.

A write the person makes, through a plugin command, to one file they chose is hand-editing. Obsidian's core editor always permits it, and B3 does not judge it. A cell passes if some seedable setting keeps the plugin's judged writes off the machine surfaces; the cell's §4 note names the exact key and value. (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)

**B4 (OKF conformance; screen C's C5 takes the same reading).** B4 is judged at the configuration research-vault would seed, not at plugin defaults. A cell passes if some seedable key set keeps what the plugin writes OKF-conformant (`docs/agents/okf-conformance.md`). The §4 note names those keys, including `app.json` keys such as `newFileLocation`, `newFileFolderPath` and `useMarkdownLinks` where the result depends on them. A cell fails if no seedable configuration does.

**Screen A, recorded here as a Zotero property for the spec.** A3 judges two things only: (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)

- (a) the add-on does not destroy or rewrite another writer's data in Extra, tags, fields or child notes;
- (b) the add-on does not change an item's citation key by itself: no automatic re-key, no rewrite of a pinned key, and no key-format change it applies unasked.

Merging duplicates is outside A3, because the hazard is Zotero's default behaviour rather than a property of any add-on. On the production Zotero profile every merge path bypasses Better BibTeX's consolidation, Zotero's own Merge pane included:

- The native pane calls `mergeItems.mjs` directly (`duplicatesMergePane.js:34`, `:203-211`).
- `Zotero.Items.merge` is a deprecated shim over the same module (`items.js:1037-1041`; Zotero @`ca61760`).
- BBT's merge shim acts only when an extraMerge pref is on. `extraMergeCitekeys`, `extraMergeTeX` and `extraMergeCSL` ship false and are unset in the profile's `prefs.js` (BBT xpi `defaults/preferences/defaults.js:54-56`; `better-bibtex.ts:96-163` @`f3c9299`).
- The only trace a merge leaves is `dc:replaces` (`mergeItems.mjs:426-477`), which `research_vault/lifecycle.py:33-83` reads.

A merge-capable add-on is judged on (a) and (b) like any other. Source: screen A evidence §7 item 3 (`2026-10-03-substrate-components-design-zotero-screen-evidence.md:1405-1415`).

**Readings the normalisation applied where the ruling is silent.** These are not rulings, and the cells named in §4 turn on them:

- **CLI handlers count as URI-class external writes (superseded 2026-10-04).** This covers Obsidian-CLI handlers that write a caller-named or caller-placed file: File recovery, Templates, Templater and QuickAdd B3. The CLI on/off switch is held per machine, outside `.obsidian/`.
- **A confirm-per-update modal does not confine (superseded 2026-10-04).** `alwaysUpdateLinks` `false` was not counted as confining in Files, Note composer and Auto Note Mover B3. Longform B3 was passed with the same modal as its gate.
- **Disabling or exiling output counts as confinement (superseded 2026-10-04).** This covers Local REST API B3 with both servers off, Pandoc Plugin B3 and B4 with `outputFolder` outside the vault, and Auto Note Mover B3 with no move rules. The verifiers asked that one ruling decide them together.
- **B4 exempts a location the person chose.** Quick switcher with a typed path, the Files folder-menu New note, and a vault-wide `type` delete in Properties view were all left pass on this carve-out, which the B4 ruling does not state.
- **The inbox exemption is narrower in the register than in the code.** The register row `okf-conformance.md:36` declines only rule 1, and `stamp.py:13-18` refuses a parseable block that lacks `type`. `structure.py:78-79` skips both rules for `inbox/`. Agent Client B4 and Tag Wrangler B4 were judged against the register row.
- **The flat parser discharges rule 1.** `frontmatter.py:166-169` was taken as rule 1's discharge for TaskNotes B4 and Excalidraw B4.
- **Hard-coded wikilinks are deviations.** A wikilink a plugin writes in a fixed form counts as an unregistered OKF §6.1 deviation (`okf-conformance.md:41`): Format converter and Unique note creator B4.

### Second ruling (2026-10-04): judged on what it does by itself

On 2026-10-04 the operator ruled again, and a second normalisation pass re-judged B3 for all 71 rows under that ruling, with a desk worker and then an adversarial verifier per family. Where the verifier refuted the re-judgement, the verifier's result is the one recorded, and the §4 line says so. In §3, **§** marks a B3 cell whose result this pass changed; existing † and ‡ are kept. §4 carries one line per changed cell and per unchanged cell whose basis or deciding key moved, each beginning "§ 2026-10-04 by-itself". The other musts were not re-judged. This ruling supersedes the first pass's invocation readings for B3: the B3 paragraph above and the readings below it marked "(superseded 2026-10-04)". The machine-surface roster stands.

**The principle.** A component is judged on what it does by itself: when an item is added or a file created, on startup or load, on a schedule or sweep, on open, in the background, through a notifier or event hook that reacts to any writer, and remotely, meaning a REST, MCP, URI or CLI handler, a sync, a git pull, or any other request that the person in the app is not making at that moment. What the person deliberately does through the component counts as the person's own edit, the same as editing by hand, and is not judged: a command or menu action the person runs on items or files they selected, a button the person presses, and a bulk change the person confirms in a prompt, since a confirm or preview dialog is deliberate whatever its scope. A component passes if a setting turns the counted behaviour off. For an Obsidian plugin that is a seedable `data.json`, `app.json` or core-plugin setting, and the §4 line names its key and value. Disabling the component outright is not a confining setting. (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling; its last sentence stands)

**B3** (and screen C's C1, which takes the same reading) is now: the plugin does not by itself write into a machine surface. The same ruling restates screen A's A3 as (a) the add-on does not by itself destroy or rewrite another writer's data in Extra, tags, fields or child notes, and (b) the add-on does not by itself change an existing citation key, where filling an empty key is not a change; merging stays outside A3. The A3 paragraph above is the first pass's statement and is left as it was; screen A's record carries the second ruling. (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling; merging stays outside A3)

**Readings the first pass left unruled, now settled by the principle.**

- CLI, URI, REST and MCP handlers are remote and counted. This settles the first pass's CLI reading the same way: File recovery, Templates, Templater and QuickAdd stay fail. It also decides Bases (`base:create`), Local REST API, Homepage's `homepage` handler, and the Daily notes and Unique note creator handlers, which pass on their folder keys.
- A confirm modal is deliberate and is not counted. Files and Note composer move to pass; Longform's scene-rename modal no longer conflicts with them; Format converter, whose vault-wide run starts only from a confirm dialog, moves to pass. (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)
- Output sent outside the vault is confined (Pandoc Plugin's `outputFolder`; Publish's uploads). Disabling is not confining, so Local REST API with both servers off moves to fail.
- A location the person chooses in a dialog is deliberate (Enhancing Export's export dialog; Importer's per-run destination). (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)
- One gesture that fans out into many files (the `B-core-1` reading, §1.1, §7.1) is the person's own edit when it is a command, menu action or confirmed bulk change. Tag Wrangler moves to pass, and Bases, Canvas and Quadro now fail only on what they do by themselves. (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)

**Readings this pass applied where the second ruling is silent.** These are not rulings, and the §4 lines name the cells that turn on them:

- A per-call agent approval prompt, or an edit preview the person accepts, is a confirm dialog (Claudian, Copilot). (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)
- A Pull the person runs is counted, because the ruling names a git pull as remote (Git).
- A setting that works only on desktop does not turn a behaviour off when the plugin also loads on mobile (Git `syncMethod`).
- A setting whose only effect is to stop the plugin's function disables it rather than confining it (Local REST API with both servers off; Templates with no `folder`; File recovery with a very large `intervalMinutes`).
- A vault-wide command the person runs with no confirm is deliberate (Excalidraw convert-all). (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)
- A plugin settings file outside `.obsidian/` that the vault template can seed counts as the plugin's seedable setting (Claudian's `.claudian/claudian-settings.json`).
- Choosing a backend or chain is the person changing a setting (Copilot). (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)
- Each counted behaviour is judged on its own writes: a remote handler whose own write is the plugin's `data.json` is not a machine-surface write, even when it can clear the confining key (Remotely Save). (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)
- A note the person turned into the plugin's own gets no exemption: a by-itself rewrite of it is counted (Longform, by the verifier; the same shape as Quadro and Agent Client).

**A fact for the spec, outside every row.** Obsidian registers its own app-level CLI handlers, among them create, append, prepend, move, rename, delete, `property:set`, `property:remove`, task, command and eval (`app.js:1@byte 1593056` to `@byte 1653050`). They belong to no core plugin: a plugin's remote surface was taken to be only the handlers it registers itself through the internal-plugin wrapper (`@byte 2922347`). With the per-machine CLI switch on (Electron IPC, `@byte 3478003`), that core block can write into machine surfaces from outside the app, and nothing in `.obsidian/` gates it. Its `command` handler (`@byte 1606805`) runs a plugin command the way the person would: it opens Format converter's modal, for example, but cannot press Start.

**What changed.** Eight B3 cells changed: six from fail to pass (Files, Format converter, Note composer, Claudian, Copilot, Tag Wrangler) and two from pass to fail (Local REST API, Longform). The verifier refuted the re-judgement on three cells: Longform, where the result flipped, and Copilot and TaskNotes, where the result held on a different key set. This record stated no per-must B3 tally before this pass, so none is recounted.

### Third ruling (2026-10-04): trigger-neutral, right by construction

On 2026-10-04 the operator ruled a third time, and a third normalisation re-judged B3 for all 71 rows under that ruling, with a desk worker and then an adversarial verifier per family: `B-core` for the 31 core rows, `B-top20` for the 20 top-20 rows, and `B-rest` for the 20 seed, search-find and floor rows. Where a verifier refuted the re-judgement, the verifier's result is the one recorded, and the §4 line says so. In §3, **∆** marks a B3 cell whose result this pass changed; existing †, ‡ and § are kept. §4 carries one line per changed cell, and one per unchanged cell whose basis, deciding key or cite moved, each beginning "∆ 2026-10-04 third ruling". In those lines a core cite `app.js:1@byte N` is a byte offset, and a cite marked as a character offset is a `B-core-1` character offset (§1.1). The other musts were not re-judged; B4 stays judged at the configuration research-vault would seed. This ruling supersedes the first and second rulings' invocation readings and every reasoning that a write is acceptable because it can be recovered; each is marked "(superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)" above and kept. The machine-surface roster (`hooks/pretooluse_guard.py:11-23`) and the exclusion of merging from A3 stand.

**The standard.** Every write or removal path a component offers is judged alike, whatever starts it: an automatic or on-add write, a notifier, a schedule, a remote route, a menu command, a button, or a change the person confirms in a preview or dialog. A confirmation, a preview, a backup, an undo or restore command, and history nobody monitors change no result. A component has to be right by construction:

- Writes (screen A's A3): every value an add-on writes into a field, Extra, a tag or a child note comes from an authoritative source looked up by identifier (a DOI resolved through doi.org, Crossref or DataCite; a PMID or PMCID from NCBI; an ISBN; an arXiv ID), or is a format change that keeps all the information, such as the case of a case-insensitive identifier, a short DOI expanded to the long one, or reversible punctuation and dash normalisation. A value obtained by fuzzy matching, a query hit taken without an identifier check, a guess from the title or another heuristic fails, even when it only fills an empty field.
- Removals (A3): it blanks, truncates, trashes, deletes or strips only what a definite test shows invalid (an exact placeholder token, a file that does not exist, a link to nothing), or an exact duplicate of information kept on the same item. A heuristic or pattern-match removal fails, and so does removing something merely redundant.
- Identity (A3): it does not change an existing citation key, except where research-vault already detects and repairs the change (lifecycle classifies a re-key and propagate maps old to new), so a re-key passes; filling an empty key passes.
- An add-on passes only if a setting removes every failing path from every trigger; a failing path still reachable from a menu command or a remote route keeps the cell a fail. Screen A's record carries that judgement.

**B3** (and screen C's C1, which takes the same reading): every write path into a machine surface fails, whoever triggers it, unless an existing research-vault mechanism detects that write, or a seedable setting removes the path from every trigger. The §4 line names the detecting mechanism with a cite, or the setting's key and value. There is no hand-edit exemption. (superseded 2026-10-04, fourth ruling)

**Readings this pass applied where the third ruling is silent.** These are not rulings; the §4 lines name the cells that turn on them. The families applied these alike:

- Disabling still does not confine. `core-plugins.json` `"<id>": false`, and a setting whose only effect is to stop the plugin working, disable rather than confine: the third ruling withdrew neither the second ruling's sentence nor the second pass's reading (Local REST API, Auto Note Mover; §7.55).
- A write counts as detected if a commit-closing verify check catches it at the vault pre-commit hook, or if a warn-tier lint files it into the review queue (append-only). A path detected in part fails on its undetected part, and the §4 line names that part.
- Obsidian's own editor and its app-level CLI handlers belong to no core plugin. A plugin's writes into the active editor, and through its embeds or popovers, are its own.
- Obsidian's link update after `fileManager.renameFile` counts as the path of the plugin that called it (Files, Note composer, Longform, Auto Note Mover).
- Publish, Sync, Git and Remotely Save are judged with an account or remote connected.
- A write into the open note or a chosen folder reaches wiki/ and fulltext/, and research-vault reports it there only if it breaks a type, a link or a citation. Every pass-to-fail cell in §3.2 turns on this reading (`B-top20`); `B-core` and `B-rest` judged the same way.

The families split on two readings. Both splits are recorded here, not resolved:

- Fulltext/ detection. `B-top20` counted a default worktree verify (the post-capture run and the publish gate) as detection for fulltext/, which the commit gate never sees, and Kanban's pass rests on it. `B-core` and `B-rest` treated fulltext/ writes the commit gate never sees as undetected.
- A route that rewrites a confining key. `B-top20` held that a remote route that rewrites the confining key defeats it, so Remotely Save fails; that replaces the second pass's Remotely Save reading. `B-rest` applied the replaced reading to Homepage's Set to active file command, treating it as a settings change. Its verifier found that the hinge does not decide the cell at `"kind": "Workspace"` or `"Nothing"`, so Homepage's pass holds there either way (§4.4).

**Superseded reasoning in §4.** Earlier §4 lines that rest a B3 result on a confirm or preview, on a file or location the person chose, or on capture regenerating a write away (for example Canvas's narrow-to-block bound, Advanced URI's desk basis and Note Refactor's link-back) are superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling. They are kept as the record of the earlier passes.

**What changed.** 33 B3 cells changed, all from pass to fail; none moved from fail to pass. Core (15): Audio recorder, Bookmarks, Files, Footnotes view, Format converter, Graph view, Note composer, Outgoing links, Outline, Page preview, Properties view, Publish, Quick switcher, Slash commands and Unique note creator. Top-20 (12): Excalidraw, Dataview, Tasks, Advanced Tables, Claudian, Copilot, Remotely Save, Editing Toolbar, Omnisearch, TaskNotes, Importer and Outliner. Seed, search finds and floor (6): Callout Manager, Note Refactor, Footnotes, Enhancing Export, Auto Note Mover and Tag Wrangler. One verifier refuted its re-judgement: Kanban, where the result held at pass on a corrected basis that its §4 line gives with the hinge. Other verifiers corrected a basis or a cite without refuting the result; their §4 lines say so. B3 tally after this pass (pre-fourth-ruling): 25 pass and 46 fail of 71 (§3.1 11 and 20; §3.2 5 and 15; §3.3 1 and 3; §3.4 4 and 6; §3.5 4 and 2). Before it (pre-third-ruling, counted from §3 as it stood; this record stated no B3 tally earlier): 58 pass and 13 fail.

### Fourth ruling (2026-10-04): beyond editing the open note

On 2026-10-04 the operator ruled a fourth time, for B3 and screen C's C1 only. A fourth reading then re-derived B3 for all 71 rows under that ruling, with a desk worker and then an adversarial verifier per family (`B-core`, `B-top20` and `B-rest`, as in the third normalisation). Where a verifier refuted the re-derivation, the verifier's result is the one recorded, and the §4 line or the paragraph below says so. In §3, **◇** marks a B3 cell whose result this reading changed; existing marks are kept. §4 carries one line per changed cell, each beginning "◇ 2026-10-04 fourth ruling"; in those lines a core cite `app.js:1@byte N` is a byte offset (§1.1). The other musts were not re-judged. This ruling supersedes the third ruling's B3 clause, marked above; the machine-surface roster (`hooks/pretooluse_guard.py:11-23`) stands.

**The ruling.** A plugin is judged only on what it adds beyond editing the note the person has open. Editing that note is the editor's ordinary behaviour and is not judged, whoever does it: inserting at the cursor, toggling a checkbox, formatting, changing the note's own frontmatter, or inserting a citation into it. research-vault's detection of machine-surface changes covers those edits; the spec is designing that detection to close the gaps recorded in §7.57. Four kinds of path are counted:

- writes into a machine surface that are background, automatic, scheduled, on create or on open;
- bulk or vault-wide rewrites across notes other than the open one, such as a tag or property rename across files or a find-and-replace across files;
- writing to, creating, or moving into a machine-surface note other than the open one;
- remote routes: REST, MCP, URI and CLI handlers, sync, a git pull or merge, and import tools that write files.

Obsidian's own vault operations (a rename with its link update in other notes, a move, a delete, and File recovery's restore) are the app's behaviour. They are recorded as an app property, not as any plugin's, the way Zotero merges are (#183), and a core plugin that only puts a button on them adds nothing counted. A cell passes if a seedable setting removes every counted path from every trigger; the §4 line names the key and value.

**Third-ruling readings this replaces for B3.** Three readings applied under the third ruling no longer decide a B3 cell; they are kept above as the record of that pass. A plugin's writes into the active editor are open-note editing, not a judged path of the plugin. Obsidian's link update after `fileManager.renameFile` is app behaviour, not the calling plugin's path (Files, Note composer, Longform, Auto Note Mover). Detection no longer passes a counted path, because the ruling names only a seedable setting (Excalidraw's file-menu drawing and Kanban, whose third-ruling bases rested on detection; Longform).

**Readings this reading applied where the fourth ruling is silent.** These are not rulings; the families applied them alike, and the cells named turn on them:

- Creation is counted. The counted paths include creating a note other than the open one, and the list of app operations does not name creation. Bookmarks, Files, Graph view, Quick switcher and Outgoing links fail only on a create through `openLinkText` or `fileManager.createNewFile`; if creating a file on following a link or from a menu is read as an Obsidian vault operation, all five pass with no key. The editor's own link click goes through the same `openLinkText` path (`app.js:1@byte 3046869`).
- The open note is the note in a workspace leaf. A pinned or grouped sidebar pane keeps its file after the person moves on, and that file counts as open (Backlinks, Outgoing links, Outline, Footnotes view, the File properties pane; `app.js:1@byte 2676300`). A note shown only in a hover popover or a canvas card embed does not count as open (Page preview; Canvas paths (b) and (e)). If it did, Page preview would pass, and Canvas would still fail on its own rename hook.
- A handler the plugin registers itself is its own remote route, even when its only target is the active file; a core app-level handler is Obsidian's (§2A Second ruling, "A fact for the spec"). Templates' `template:insert` handler therefore stays counted, while the core `command` handler that can start a recording is not Audio recorder's. If "whoever does it" is read to cover a remote insert into the active file, Templates passes.
- A named app operation covers every route to it. File recovery's restore is named, so its plugin-registered `history:restore` CLI handler, which writes a file's own stored snapshot back as the modal does, adds nothing counted. If a plugin-registered remote route onto a named app operation counts as a remote route, File recovery fails with no key. This reading and the previous one pull against each other; both are recorded, not resolved.
- A toggle whose write lands in another file is counted. A checkbox clicked in a query result rewrites the task's own file, often a note other than the open one (Dataview, Tasks, TaskNotes). The `B-top20` verifier recorded that all three pass if the ruling's checkbox clause covers such a toggle; Tasks and TaskNotes also pass if query text the person writes is not a trigger.
- A file or folder picked in a dialog has no exemption (Note Refactor's append target, Enhancing Export's export directory, Importer's destination).
- Disabling still does not confine (§7.55). `core-plugins.json` `"<id>": false`, or a setting whose only effect is to stop the plugin, leaves a counted path in place (Local REST API). Homepage's `"kind": "Nothing"` may fall under this reading, so `"kind": "Workspace"` is the setting that decides Homepage.
- A remote route that rewrites the confining key defeats it (Remotely Save, as under the third ruling); judged on the route's own write alone, the cell would pass on `ignorePaths`.

**An app property, outside every row.** `fileManager.createNewFile` passes a caller-supplied name through without resolving `..`, and the adapter's recursive folder creation resolves it at the system call. A traversal name (Unique note creator's CLI, `base:create`, a typed Quick switcher name, an Omnisearch query) can therefore leave an empty directory under a machine folder before the hidden-path test makes the create throw; no note is written (`app.js:1@byte 1775859-1776700`, `@byte 554185`, `@byte 566809`).

**What changed.** 15 B3 cells changed: 14 from fail to pass and one from pass to fail. Core (7, all to pass): Audio recorder, File recovery, Footnotes view, Outline, Properties view, Slash commands and Unique note creator. Top-20 (5): Advanced Tables, Editing Toolbar, Omnisearch and Outliner to pass, and Kanban to fail. Seed, search finds and floor (3, all to pass): Callout Manager, Footnotes and Auto Note Mover. The verifiers refuted the re-derivation on four cells, and their results are recorded. On File recovery the re-derivation kept fail and the verifier passed it on the named restore. On Outgoing links, Templates and Note Refactor the re-derivation passed the cell and the verifier kept it at fail. Those three cells carry no ◇ and no new §4 line, because the result did not change, but each now fails on a path its third-ruling line does not give as deciding:

- Outgoing links: clicking an unresolved item in its Links list calls `openLinkText`, which creates the file and its folders at a path-qualified link, so a link to a log/ or wiki/ path creates a machine-surface note other than the open one. No key removes it. The Link button, which writes the pane's own file, is no longer judged (`app.js:1@byte 3431100-3431500`, `@byte 1539800-1540200`, `@byte 3419187`, `@byte 3430240`).
- Templates: the plugin-registered `template:insert` CLI handler inserts a named template into the active editor at a remote caller's request, and no confining key exists. Insert template, the date and time inserts and the ribbon item are no longer judged (`app.js:1@byte 3698549-3699300`, `@byte 3700470-3700720`, `@byte 3701682`).
- Note Refactor: the content-only extract-selection command (default hotkey Mod+Shift+C) and the content-only split command open a modal that lists every Markdown file in the vault and appends to the one chosen through `vault.modify`, wherever it lives, with no key. The source-note replacement is no longer judged, and the new-note path stays confined by `data.json` `"newFileLocation": 2` with `"customFolder": "inbox/refactor"` (`src/main.ts:50-59`, `:74-77`, `:186-199`; `src/note-modal.ts:26`, `:47-52`; `src/modal-note-creation.ts:29-50` @`f403996`).

In the other unchanged fail cells, earlier §4 lines still name paths that are now open-note editing or app operations, for example Bookmarks' block-id insert, Files' rename, move and delete, Note composer's selection replacement and link update, Longform's scene rename and trash, and Excalidraw's embed at the cursor. Those parts are superseded 2026-10-04, fourth ruling; each cell's fail rests on its remaining counted paths. Excalidraw's now are the file-menu Create new into a right-clicked folder, or into a right-clicked file's folder, convert-all, and convert-card-to-file at a typed path (`src/core/managers/CommandManager.ts:190-227`, `:2552-2565`; `src/core/managers/FileManager.ts:140-153`; `src/view/ExcalidrawView.ts:6909-6960` @`128a9ef`). Verifiers also corrected bases without changing a result. Advanced URI's file-menu Copy Advanced URI writes a UID into the right-clicked file, not only the active note, a counted path confined by `"useUID": false`, the default (`src/main.ts:273-300`; `src/tools.ts:106-116` @`2f9b9a7`). Enhancing Export's export directory is picked through the system folder picker, not typed, since the dialog's directory field is disabled (`src/ui/ExportDialog.tsx:107` @`4b63d2c`).

B3 tally after this reading: 38 pass and 33 fail of 71 (§3.1 18 and 13; §3.2 8 and 12; §3.3 2 and 2; §3.4 5 and 5; §3.5 5 and 1). The third ruling's tally above is pre-fourth-ruling.

### Inclusive rule and five workflows (2026-10-04)

On 2026-10-04 the operator decided how Obsidian plugins are selected and settled three readings the fourth reading had left open. A classification pass then re-judged B2 for all 71 rows against five workflows, applied the settled readings to B3, and sorted every row into one class. Each family had a classifier and then an adversarial verifier: `B-core` for the 31 core rows, `B-top20` for the 20 top-20 rows, and `B-rest` for the 20 seed, search-find and floor rows. Where a verifier refuted the classifier, the verifier's result is the one recorded. In §3, **⊕** marks a B2 or B3 cell whose result this pass changed; existing marks are kept. The pass adds no §4 line: §9 gives each row's class, workflows, settings, condition and reason. B1, B4 and B5 were not re-judged. The spec records (a) to (c) and the licence point of (b) in its rulings 5 to 8; it does not record (d).

**(a) Selection rule.** A plugin is included if it aids one of the operator's five workflows and causes no harm. The five workflows are:

1. research: question, literature search, capture, compile, synthesis, draft, submit;
2. analysis and reports: tables, queries, charts, and reports exported to docx, pdf or html;
3. personal knowledge management: finding, linking, organising, splitting and capturing notes, tags, tasks, navigation;
4. long-form writing: drafting, manuscript assembly, footnotes, word goals, focus, export;
5. daily logs: a person-owned diary in `daily/`, kept with core Daily notes, with Calendar or Periodic navigation and weekly and monthly reviews.

The step map in §1's shared facts gains two steps, for (2) and (3). B2, "serves a step or the floor", is re-judged against all five workflows. A §3 B2 cell whose result changed names the workflows it rests on by number; a cell whose result held keeps its step-map basis, and §9 names the workflows for every row.

**(b) Harm.** Harm is an unconfined counted path under the fourth ruling, an OKF conformance break (B4), a failure to load (B1), or, in screen C, breaking key resolution (C2). The counted paths are background, automatic, scheduled, on-create or on-open writes into a machine surface; bulk or vault-wide rewrites of notes other than the open one; writes to another machine-surface note; and remote routes. A counted path is confined when a seedable setting removes it. A redistribution-licence failure (B5) is not harm. It decides only how an included plugin is declared: SEED, vendored into the public repository's seeded `.obsidian/`, or RECOMMEND, where the person installs it from Obsidian's community browser, it is declared with its settings, and doctor observes it.

**(c) Three readings, settled.**

- (i) Creating a note by following a link or from a menu (`openLinkText`, or `fileManager.createNewFile` on a link or a menu create) is Obsidian's own vault operation. It is app behaviour, not the plugin's path.
- (ii) A direct edit to one specific note shown in another view, such as a Bases table cell, a checkbox in a query result or a property edited from a pane, is editing that note and is not judged.
- (iii) A handler that only inserts into the open note, such as Templates' `template:insert`, is open-note editing whoever triggers it.

These replace three of the fourth reading's readings. (i) replaces "Creation is counted", so Bookmarks, Files, Graph view, Quick switcher and Outgoing links pass. (ii) replaces "A toggle whose write lands in another file is counted" and the popover part of "The open note is the note in a workspace leaf", so Dataview, Tasks, TaskNotes and Page preview pass; Canvas still fails on its own rename hook. (iii) replaces "A handler the plugin registers itself is its own remote route" for a handler that only inserts, so Templates passes. A plugin-registered handler that creates or writes any other note stays counted (Bases' `base:create`, Templater, QuickAdd). (c) does not settle the fourth reading's "A named app operation covers every route to it", and File recovery's pass still rests on it.

**(d) Fixes in flight are conditions.** Two research-vault fixes in flight count as conditions, not passes: #194 (verify walks `.obsidian/` and the other dot-directories, and the flat frontmatter parser rejects nested YAML) and the type map's ability to type new folders. A row that fails only on one of them is CONDITIONAL, and §9 names the issue.

**Classes.** Each row takes exactly one class:

- SEED: it aids a workflow; B1 passes, or is needs-live only for a host-specific or not-yet-run load; no unconfined harm path remains with the named settings; B4 passes with the named settings; and the licence permits redistribution.
- RECOMMEND: as SEED, but the licence forbids or does not establish redistribution, or the plugin needs a setting the person must make that the seed cannot carry.
- CONDITIONAL: it would be SEED or RECOMMEND once a named research-vault fix under (d) lands.
- EXCLUDE: it aids no workflow, or it has an unconfined harm path, or it breaks OKF with no setting and no pending fix.

For a core plugin, SEED means on in `core-plugins.json` and EXCLUDE means off.

**Readings the verifiers applied where the decisions are silent.** These are not rulings; §9 names the rows that turn on them:

- A menu create that writes the new note's initial content before the note opens is part of the (c)(i) create (Kanban's new board; Templater's folder-menu Create new note from template). Read the other way, Kanban and Templater are EXCLUDE.
- QuickAdd's file-menu Apply template on a right-clicked note is a (c)(ii) direct edit of one note. A note picked by its path from a vault-wide list, whose content is not shown, is not: Note Refactor's append target and Note composer's merge destination stay counted.
- Obsidian's command-line interface switch is held per machine, outside `.obsidian/`, and stays off until the person enables it (Obsidian help at `obsidian-help@9cf8c29`, paraphrased). Keeping it off is a setting the person must make that the seed cannot carry, so a row whose only remaining counted path is a CLI handler the plugin registers itself is RECOMMEND (Templater, QuickAdd). With the switch on, Obsidian's own app-level handlers already reach the roster (§2A Second ruling, "A fact for the spec"), so the switch is one vault-wide precondition, which doctor cannot observe from `.obsidian/`.
- (b)'s bulk-rewrite and remote-route bullets are read as scoped to machine surfaces, as B3 is. Properties view's All-properties rename and delete, and Daily notes' remote handlers, pass on that reading; read unscoped, Properties view is EXCLUDE.
- A load that fails only on some hosts is host-specific (Novel word count, which fails under `LANG=C.UTF-8`, §4.5).

**Where the decisions and the spec's rulings differ.** (b) counts an OKF break as harm. The spec's ruling 6 names harm as an unconfined counted path, a load failure or a destructive write, and its ruling 7 accepts an OKF break given a deviation-register row and a verify exemption or type mapping. Three rows are EXCLUDE on B4 alone: Tasks, Unique note creator and Web viewer. Under ruling 7 each would instead turn on a register row and an exemption. The `B-top20` verifier raised this for Tasks; the same holds for the other two.

**What changed.** 37 cells changed, in 35 rows. Of the 26 B2 cells, 25 moved from fail to pass and one from pass to fail:

- core (9): Audio recorder, Format converter, Publish, Random note, Slash commands, Slides, Sync, Unique note creator and Web viewer;
- top-20 (13): Excalidraw, Tasks, Advanced Tables, Git, Claudian, Copilot, Remotely Save, Iconize, Editing Toolbar, Omnisearch, TaskNotes, Importer and Outliner;
- seed (3): Homepage, Agent Client and Callout Manager;
- pass to fail (1): Contextual Typography, where the verifier refuted the classifier: current Minimal no longer keys its width rules on the plugin's classes, and the seed ships no theme or snippet.

All 11 changed B3 cells moved from fail to pass: core (7) Bookmarks, Files, Graph view, Outgoing links, Page preview, Quick switcher and Templates; top-20 (4) Dataview, Tasks, Kanban and TaskNotes. Templater's and QuickAdd's B3 cells stay fail, because no seedable key removes their CLI handlers. The verifiers refuted the class on three rows: Templater and QuickAdd, from EXCLUDE to RECOMMEND, and Contextual Typography, from SEED to EXCLUDE. B2 tally after this pass: 67 pass and 4 fail of 71 (Style Settings, Minimal Theme Settings, Local REST API, Contextual Typography). B3 tally after this pass: 49 pass and 22 fail of 71 (§3.1 25 and 6; §3.2 12 and 8; §3.3 2 and 2; §3.4 5 and 5; §3.5 5 and 1). Classes: 39 SEED, 4 RECOMMEND, 2 CONDITIONAL, 26 EXCLUDE (§9).

## 3. Results

Each must cell shows the verified result. **†** follows the verifier's `refuted` flag: it marks a cell where the family's verifier refuted the desk, and §4 gives the desk value and says whether the result itself changed or only its basis or cite did. Verifier corrections that left the flag unset (for example Backlinks B3, Note composer B3, Tasks B3, Git B3) carry no † and appear in §4 only. An exit line marked **†** is the verifier's corrected line; the desk's line is in §4. B1 cells carry the desk fact (manifest `minAppVersion`; `isDesktopOnly`). Core plugins have no manifest, so their B1 cell carries the default-on state instead. B2 cells name the step or floor the result rests on (`none` for a fail).

Pins are release tag at tag commit. Core rows are pinned to Obsidian desktop 1.13.7, asset `obsidian-1.13.7.asar.gz`, and their licence is the asar's UNLICENSED declaration (`package.json:5`); only settings would be seeded.
