## 6. Live legs (pending at 2026-10-03; run 2026-10-04, §6.5)

Every live leg is tracer T5 (assembly-design.md:453, open): scaffold a research-vault vault, seed `.obsidian/plugins/<id>/{main.js,manifest.json,styles.css}` plus `community-plugins.json` and `core-plugins.json`, open it in Obsidian desktop with restricted mode off, and record the Obsidian version the tracer ran on (workers asked for this because GitHub's `latest` and the update channel differ, §1.1).

### 6.1 B1, the 65 rows of §3.1–§3.4 (pre-run; the 6 floor rows are in §6.4)

The B1 desk fact for each row is in §3. Each row's load proof and the measurement its worker attached:

**Core plugins (31).** For every row: the plugin's enabled state matches the seeded `core-plugins.json`, and the file is not rewritten on first open (§7.4). Further measurements:

- Audio recorder: record once with a literature note active; the file lands at `attachmentFolderPath` and the only other change is the embed line.
- Backlinks: after viewing a literature note's backlinks the vault tree is unchanged.
- Bases: open both seeded `.base` files; record what the `open-questions.base` formula evaluates to; resize one column and diff `system/bases/*.base`. The verifier added: open both with no gesture and diff, since a first-render write is unmeasured (code shows `migrateDisplayName` does not save on load, `@2311279`).
- Bookmarks: a seeded `bookmarks.json` loads.
- Canvas: create a canvas with a literature note active and check its location; convert a text card to a file and record where the note lands and whether it has frontmatter (traced on paper by the verifier to `app.json` `newFileLocation`).
- Command palette: seeded pinned commands appear.
- Daily notes: with a seeded `daily-notes.json`, open today's note and confirm path and template; then run the CLI's log append on the same day and confirm verify passes.
- File recovery: nothing appears in the vault after editing for longer than `intervalMinutes`.
- Files: rename a literature note a wiki page links to; the prompt appears and Don't update leaves wiki/ untouched.
- Footnotes view: enabled, it lists footnotes from a draft.
- Format converter: it stays off.
- Graph view: the seeded `graph.json` is still in force after claude-obsidian's adopt has run.
- Note composer: merge two scratch notes a wiki page links to and observe the update-links prompt (the verifier showed from code that it fires).
- Outgoing links, Outline: enabled.
- Editor status: the seeded key does not trigger a rewrite of `core-plugins.json`.
- Page preview, Publish, Quick switcher, Random note, Search, Slash commands, Slides, Templates, Unique note creator, Web viewer, Word count, Workspaces: state matches the seed, no rewrite.
- Properties view: whether the reserved `tags` property flags literature notes' object-shaped Zotero tags as a type mismatch.
- Sync: whether the default merge of concurrent frontmatter edits yields parseable YAML (also B4, §6.2).
- Tags view: whether the in-note properties editor flags the reserved `tags` key and invites a rewrite (the tag-list question was settled at desk, §4.2).

**Top-20 community plugins (20).**

- Excalidraw: load 2.28.1; create one drawing; run `verify --surface commit` and observe the okf-frontmatter row; check `compatibilityMode` behaviour.
- Templater: load 2.25.1 (needs at least 1.13.0); confirm the localStorage key `templater-local-settings` is absent on a fresh vault and a CLI-written literature note is left untouched.
- Dataview: load 0.5.68 (minAppVersion 0.13.11, last release 2025-04); record which version a fresh catalog install delivers (0.5.68 or 0.5.70).
- Tasks: with `globalFilter` seeded, a checkbox line without the filter is unchanged on click.
- Advanced Tables, Style Settings: load.
- Git: commit from Obsidian on desktop and observe that the vault pre-commit hook runs. (The desk's mobile question is settled on paper, not a live leg: isomorphic-git has no pre-commit hook, §4.3.)
- Calendar: load 1.5.10 (minAppVersion 0.9.11, unmaintained since 2021); with the daily-notes folder `log`, observe the write to log/ after a confirmed click.
- Kanban: New board opens a board view with no console error.
- Claudian: add a vault `.claude/settings.json` that enables research-vault, start a chat, ask for a Write under literature/, and see whether `pretooluse_guard.py` denies it.
- Copilot: with `autosaveChat` false, it loads and nothing appears under `copilot/` or `.copilot/` until first use (the verifier found `enableRecentConversations` and `enableSavedMemory` also govern that).
- Remotely Save: with `ignorePaths` set and no remote configured, it loads and does not sync on its own.
- Iconize: with `iconsInNotesEnabled` false and `iconPacksPath` set, nothing is written outside the config dir.
- QuickAdd: with `choices: []`, it loads and the command palette lists no choices.
- Editing Toolbar: with `ai.enabled` false the toolbar renders with no AI onboarding prompt (the verifier found this needs `ai.onboardingShown` true as well).
- Omnisearch: it loads, indexes and creates no file in the vault.
- TaskNotes: seeded with the B3 and B4 keys, nothing appears at `TaskNotes/Start Here.md` or under log/.
- Minimal Theme Settings: with the Minimal theme also seeded, it loads and writes only `appearance.json` and its `data.json`.
- Importer: one small import into a non-machine folder; nothing lands outside that folder or the attachment folder.
- Outliner: edit a list in a scratch note; no other file changes.

**Author's vault seed (4).**

- Homepage: kind File pointing at a non-machine note; restart twice; no file is created or modified.

### 6.5 Run 2026-10-04

Live legs ran on 2026-10-04 between 01:35 and 01:58 local time (UTC−5). Each result below is the leg's, after an adversarial verifier re-read the raw logs: the verifier supported all 29 screen-B results, so no screen-B result here differs from what the leg reported (at the run; superseded ¤: a post-run check moved one, Novel word count B1, from the leg's fail to needs-live, because the failure depends on the host's process locale, §4.5, §7.42; the other 28 results are as the leg reported). Where the verifier tightened an observation (Editor status, Daily notes, Workspaces, Calendar, Contextual Typography, Footnotes view), the row's ◊ line in §4 uses its version. ◊ in §3 and §4 marks these results. Raw logs are under `/tmp/lane23-live/obsidian/logs/`, outside the repository; file names below are relative to it.

**Environment.** Obsidian desktop 1.13.7 (installer 1.12.7) under WSL from `/opt/Obsidian`, with the per-machine CLI switch on (`env.log`; `step0.log`). The scratch vault `~/rv-lane3-scratch-vault` is a research-vault scaffold (one commit, `04dc25e`). Its `.obsidian/` held only `app.json` and `appearance.json` (both `{}`), `core-plugins.json` and `workspace.json`, which 1.13.7 wrote at the vault's first open on 2026-10-03 19:00 (`step0.log`; `/tmp/lane23-live/t5-first-open.txt`; copies in `/tmp/lane23-live/obsidian/snapshot/`). research-vault seeds no `.obsidian/` today (§2.2 search log, row 9), so "first open" in the ◊ lines means Obsidian's own defaults: an object holding all 31 registered ids (§7.41).

**Method.** The leg drove the running app through the Obsidian CLI (`eval`, `plugins:restrict`, `plugins:enabled`, `dev:errors`, `dev:console`). Core rows: registration, enabled and loaded state, and command and view counts were read from the internal-plugin registry; the two rows off at first open (Footnotes view, Workspaces) were enabled and then disabled through the plugin wrapper, with `core-plugins.json` hashed at each step (`step1-core.log`). Community rows: restricted mode was left with `app.plugins.setEnable(true)` (§7.35). Then, one plugin at a time, the pinned release assets were copied into `.obsidian/plugins/<id>/`, `loadManifests` and `enablePluginAndSave` were called, the state was read back after 3 s together with `dev:errors` and `dev:console`, and the plugin was disabled with `disablePluginAndSave`. The vault tree was hashed before, after enabling and after disabling (`/tmp/lane23-live/obsidian/pl.sh`, `tree.py`; `plugin-<id>.log` and `plugin-<id>-tree-*.txt`). Asset URLs and hashes: `step2-release-assets.log`, `step2-download-hashes.txt`.

**Method gap.** This was a hot install into a running app, one plugin at a time. The T5 route in the §6 preamble, which seeds `.obsidian/plugins/<id>/` with `community-plugins.json` and `core-plugins.json` and then opens the vault cold, was not exercised, and the plugins were never loaded together. No research-vault-seeded `core-plugins.json` exists, so the first-open rewrite question (§7.4) was not tested.

**Set.** The run covered exactly the rows that have no fail in B2–B5: 18 core rows and 11 community rows.

**Not run, and what each needs.**

- B1 for the other 42 rows: 13 core rows (Audio recorder, Bases, Canvas, File recovery, Format converter, Publish, Random note, Slash commands, Slides, Sync, Templates, Unique note creator, Web viewer), the 18 top-20 rows other than Dataview and Calendar, the 4 author-seed rows, 5 search finds (Longform, Advanced URI, Pandoc Plugin, Enhancing Export, Quadro) and 2 floor rows (Typewriter Scroll, Tag Wrangler). Their §3 B1 cells stay needs-live, with no ◊. The same CLI procedure covers a load check; no human step is needed for it.

- Novel word count on a host whose language list holds only valid BCP 47 tags, such as a desktop session not started with `LANG=C.UTF-8`. It needs such a host (at the run; superseded ¤: it needs no other machine). ¤ On this host, relaunching Obsidian with `LANG` unset or `LANG=C` gives `["en-US"]`, and `LANG=en_US.UTF-8` gives `["en-US","en","en"]` in the offline reproduction, with `LANGUAGE` and `LC_ALL` unset because they take precedence. glib reads only the environment, so no locale needs generating, though `locale -a` here lists only C, C.utf8 and POSIX. Then run `enablePluginAndSave` on the same 5.0.0 assets and read back the loaded state and the console. The alternative is a Windows or macOS desktop, where Electron takes the list from the OS language preferences. The check did not relaunch the shared running app (§7.42).
- §6.2 and §6.3 (pre-run): none run. Sync's merge leg needs a person signed in to a paid Obsidian Sync account on two devices.
- §6.4's floor-configuration first open: not run.

**Restore.** At 01:58 the leg removed `.obsidian/plugins/`, `community-plugins.json`, `workspaces.json` and a root `citation-content-template.md` that a screen-C plugin wrote on load, keeping copies of those files and of the plugin-written files under `/tmp/lane23-live/obsidian/artifacts/`. It copied the four first-open files back (sha256 equal to the snapshot), removed the `enable-plugin-<appId>` localStorage key so that restricted mode is on again (`plugins:restrict` reports `on`), removed its in-memory `navigator.languages` override and window globals, detached its CDP debugger and stopped its echo server. `git status --porcelain=v1 --ignored` then matched step 0 (`step4-restore.log`). The verifier reported re-checking the vault and the running instance independently at 02:02 and found the restore held; it wrote no file, so that check is recorded only in its report. The other vault registered in this Obsidian, `u3-plug-retry`, was not touched.

## 7. Open questions the workers raised

**7.1 One reading of B3.** `B-core-1` stated a gesture rule (§1.1) and asked the spec to confirm or replace it. The `B-community-1` verifier found its family inconsistent against any single rule: Dataview fails because a user click rewrites a file and no key turns it off, while Git (a user pull leaving conflict markers in machine-surface files), the Tasks toggle command, Advanced Tables commands and Excalidraw's file-menu create pass under a hand-edit reading. The distinction the desk drew (a remote rewrite of an existing file) was not stated as the rule. The `B-community-3` verifier noted that Importer passes on a user-chosen, unbounded output folder, the same property used to fail Local REST API. Rows whose result turns on the reading include Dataview, Git, Tasks, Advanced Tables, Excalidraw, Bases (b, e), Canvas, Claudian (per-call approval), QuickAdd, Omnisearch, Importer, Local REST API (servers off), Advanced URI and Pandoc Plugin. Resolved 2026-10-03: see Standards as applied. Resolved 2026-10-04 by the second ruling.

**7.2 One reading of B4.** Excalidraw was judged at defaults; Calendar, Periodic Notes, Quick switcher, Note Refactor, Omnisearch and TaskNotes were judged under seeded keys (`B-community-1` verifier). The `B-community-2` verifier found Kanban failed for a typeless `.md` at the core new-note location while Omnisearch passed for an empty, frontmatter-less `.md` at the same location; both depend on the seeded `app.json` `newFileLocation` and `newFileFolderPath`, and Kanban's folder-menu path and Omnisearch's `current` mode both escape that setting. Resolved 2026-10-03: see Standards as applied.

**7.3 Is all of system/ a machine surface?** The shared facts say yes. Vault `AGENTS.md:7` names only `system/bibliography.json` and `system/propagations/` under system/, and `hooks/pretooluse_guard.py:11-24` protects only those, since `system/templates` is hand-edited. Bases' B3 depends on the answer (it rewrites `system/bases/*.base` whenever a view changes); no other row does. Resolved 2026-10-03: see Standards as applied.

**7.4 Shape and drift of the seeded `core-plugins.json`.** 1.13.7 reads it as an object of id to boolean; the array form is migrated through `core-plugins-migration.json`. Any registered id missing from the file falls back to `defaultOn` and makes Obsidian rewrite the file on first open (`app.js:1@2918891`, `@2919660`; `@byte 2924434`, `2924854`), which immediately trips assembly spec §15 item 17's drift check; toggling any plugin rewrites it again. The set of keys that avoids a first-open rewrite depends on build flags, because `publish`, `sync` and `webviewer` load conditionally. Sync, when it merges `core-plugins.json`, forces `"sync": true` into it (`@3682372`), so a seeded false can be reverted from another device. Leaving the `sync` key out enables Sync, which is on by default.

**7.5 Who owns daily-log in Obsidian.** Core Daily notes is on by default (`@3352677`), so every fresh vault has it unless the seed says false. Pointing its folder at log/ with format `YYYY-MM-DD` writes the CLI's machine path (`publish.py:422`); pointing it elsewhere starts a second day log beside the vault's own. The floor is bound to daily-log (assembly spec:146), yet the floor's own vault disables the plugin. Calendar, Periodic Notes (through its fallback), Homepage's Daily Note kind, TaskNotes' mini-calendar and timeblocking, and Advanced URI's `daily=true` all write wherever the core daily-notes folder points.

**7.6 Who owns `app.json`.** Several plugins obey keys no single plugin owns: `alwaysUpdateLinks` (default false), `newFileLocation` (root) and `newFileFolderPath` (`/`), `attachmentFolderPath` (`/`), `openBehavior` (`""`), `useMarkdownLinks` (false), `newLinkFormat` (shortest), `trashOption` (system), `userIgnoreFilters` (null), `deleteUnlinkedAttachments` (ask); defaults at `app.js:1@1246673-1247433`. Recorded facts: routing new untyped notes into inbox/ (`newFileLocation` `"folder"`, `newFileFolderPath` `"inbox"`) is the one key that keeps notes created by Files, Note composer, Canvas, Quick switcher and Omnisearch OKF-clean; with `useMarkdownLinks` false, Audio recorder, Backlinks, Outgoing links and Note composer insert wikilinks, which the deviation register covers only for claim and stance links (`okf-conformance.md:41`), and `useMarkdownLinks` true is the key that keeps inserted links in OKF §6.1's markdown form; Quadro overwrites `useMarkdownLinks` and `propertiesInDocument` at runtime; the floor's `app.json` sets `alwaysUpdateLinks` true, `newFileLocation` `"current"`, `attachmentFolderPath` `meta/attachments` and `userIgnoreFilters` `["meta/"]` (`a3d22d0a .obsidian/app.json:2,6,19,20-22`), the opposite of several named keys; claude-obsidian 2.2.0's adopt seeds `.obsidian/app.json` (`newLinkFormat` absolute; `userIgnoreFilters` `.raw/` and `.vault-meta/`) and `graph.json` and skips files that exist (pre-lane-2 skills evidence:65), so values the vault wants are carried only if research-vault's seed, which scaffold writes first, carries them. Which family or screen owns `app.json` is open.

**7.7 The `userIgnoreFilters` trade-off.** It is the one key that keeps Properties view's vault-wide rename and delete, and Backlinks' Link button, off the machine surfaces. The same filter drops those files from Search, ranks them lower in Quick switcher, and removes them from Tags view and the All properties list. The proposed list omits root `log.md`.

**7.8 Vendoring mechanics.** research-vault is MIT (`LICENSE:1`; `.claude-plugin/plugin.json:10`). Vendoring GPL-3.0 (Advanced Tables, Style Settings, Kanban, Omnisearch), AGPL-3.0 (Excalidraw, Templater, Copilot) or MPL-2.0 (Editing Toolbar, Focus Mode) artifacts means conveying minified `main.js`, a non-source form: GPL and AGPL §6 need the Corresponding Source or a §6(d) pointer that stays available, which only the upstream can guarantee for a tag; MPL §3.2 needs a source pointer. Where each licence text and source offer sits inside the seeded `.obsidian/`, and whether that is acceptable, is open. No candidate's release assets include a LICENSE (Agent Client: nor NOTICE), so a vendored seed adds them. Many bundles omit notices for MIT, ISC, BSD, Apache or BlueOak code they embed (per-row in §4), so a third-party notices file would be needed. Two pins declare a licence in `package.json` that conflicts with their LICENSE file (Excalidraw `:33`, Style Settings `:20`). TaskNotes bundles MPL-2.0 ical.js without a notice; Git bundles an LGPL-3.0-or-later snippet.

**7.9 Bundled code with no redistribution grant.** Claudian and Copilot bundle `@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk`, whose `LICENSE.md` reserves all rights and points to Anthropic's legal agreements; whether those agreements let third parties redistribute bundled SDK code is open. Remotely Save's `main.js` bundles PolyForm Strict code from `pro/`. Pandoc Plugin embeds CSS copyrighted by the Obsidian developers under a permission given only to its author. Enhancing Export embeds `neurips.sty` with no licence statement. Longform's FAFO licence grants redistribution on revocable conditions.

**7.10 Agent hosts and the guard.** research-vault's PreToolUse guard matches only Edit, Write and NotebookEdit (`hooks/hooks.json:6`; `pretooluse_guard.py:107`), so any embedded agent's Bash-tool write into literature/, log/ or wiki/ bypasses it whichever setting sources load. Non-Claude backends (Claudian's Codex, OpenCode, Grok, Pi and ACP; Copilot's opencode and Codex) never see it. Claudian loads user settings by default; Copilot's SDK backend loads all sources by the SDK's documented default and injects its own PreToolUse hook. Whether Agent Client's ACP path loads the hooks is live (§6.2).

**7.11 verify reaches seeded and plugin-owned files.** `research_vault/structure.py:36` `EXCLUDED_DIRS` does not include `.obsidian/`, and verify's walk (`verify.py:752-757`) enters dot-dirs, so any `.md` a plugin or the seed places under `.obsidian/` is checked against OKF rules 1-2; a vendored plugin folder carrying a README or changelog `.md` would close the commit gate. None of the release asset sets screened, and none of Enhancing Export's load-time resources, is an `.md`. Plugin-owned note folders (Kanban boards, `copilot/`, `.claude/commands` and skills, `Excalidraw/`, `TaskNotes/`, `Codes/` and `Extractions/`) are walked too; whether such folders are excluded or typed is open. Separately, the vault's flat-only frontmatter parser (`frontmatter.py:166-169`) rejects nested YAML that OKF §11 accepts, so any plugin writing nested YAML (Longform by construction) fails the commit gate. Every seeded `.obsidian/*.json` also hits the dotfile packaging blocker in assembly spec `:86`: the pyproject glob does not match dot-paths, and `_DOTLESS_TEMPLATE_RENAMES` (`scaffold.py:60`) renames files, not directories.

**7.12 Secrets, per-machine values and the config-dir name.** A seeded `.obsidian/` copied from a live vault would publish Local REST API's generated `apiKey` and TLS private key, TaskNotes' calendar sync tokens, and Copilot's `userId` (which would then be shared by every scaffolded vault); the author's agent-client `data.json` holds an absolute path and an env block, and obsidian-git's folder holds `obsidian_askpass.sh`. Iconize's default `iconPacksPath` hard-codes `.obsidian/icons` and Pandoc Plugin reads `<vault>/.obsidian/config` at a fixed path, while assembly spec §6.2 makes the config dir user-overridable. Claudian keeps its settings in a vault-root `.claudian/` that the template's gitignore does not list. Agent Client's transcripts under the config dir would be committed in a git-tracked vault.

**7.13 Does a one-time migration count as vault-setup?** Importer's B2 depends on it (desk pass, verifier fail).

**7.14 What counts as floor evidence.** The guide's prose and the starter vault disagree in places. Kanban: the guide's workflow does not use it, but the starter vault ships and uses a board (verifier, B2 flipped to pass). Footnotes: the comparison doc never mentions footnotes, but the starter vault ships `obsidian-footnotes` and pins a footnote command (`B-core-1`); the search-new verifier, reading the comparison doc only, called the floor half unsupported. QuickAdd's floor role is three macros that depend on Advanced URI and user scripts. Canvas, Outgoing links and Editor status pass at face value from the floor's enabled set, with no artifact showing use. Callout Manager: the floor's callout mapping is part of an import template the repository declined to copy.

**7.15 The Heppler 2026 mentions.** The `B-search-new` desk recorded Heppler 2026 mentions (Longform, Note Refactor, Pandoc Plugin, Novel word count, Focus Mode) as unverified, saying a web search on 2026-10-03 found no such setup page. The same family's search log records reading `jasonheppler.org/2026/01/08/how-i-use-obsidian-redux/`, which names those plugins. The two records disagree; neither was reconciled.

**7.16 Config-dir churn after seeding.** Writes into the seeded config dir that would show as tracked-file drift: Obsidian rewriting `core-plugins.json` (§7.4); Templater migrating a `data.json` without `data_version: 2`; Footnotes rewriting `data.json` without `settingsVersion: 2`, and pinning a property type when its prefix command runs; Remotely Save re-saving (obfuscating) `data.json` on load unless `obfuscateSettingFile` is false; Editing Toolbar saving `onboardingShown`; Enhancing Export writing lua and textemplate resources into its plugin folder on load; Longform rewriting `data.json` every session (or `sessions.json`); Novel word count's count cache when `writeCountsToFile` is true; Quadro rewriting `app.json`.

**7.17 Release cadence.** Copilot, Claudian, QuickAdd and Editing Toolbar each released within a day before this screen, so seeded versions will churn. Kanban's only release predates its move to the community archive. Calendar (2021), Periodic Notes (2021), Pandoc Plugin (2022) and Note Refactor (2023) are dormant. Dataview's store version (0.5.68 or 0.5.70) is a live leg.

**7.18 The seeded `open-questions.base` uses `file.content`**, which 1.13.7's Bases does not expose, so it is expected to error or return nothing (`B-core-1`; related to issue #161). The minimum Obsidian version for `.base` files is recorded nowhere (assembly spec:248). Outside this screen's write scope.

**7.19 Zotero tags under Obsidian's reserved `tags` key.** Literature notes store Zotero tags as `{tag, type}` mappings under `tags` (`literature_notes.py:51`; `tests/test_literature_notes.py:447`). Tags view and tag search drop them (settled at desk); whether the properties editor flags the key and invites a rewrite is live.

**7.20 Templates seam.** If `templates.json` `folder` points at `system/templates`, Obsidian's substitution fills `project.md`'s `{{TITLE}}` with the file name and leaves `{{ACTOR}}` and `{{NOW}}` literal, conflicting with `skills/project-flow/SKILL.md:48`.

**7.21 Workspaces and the seed gitignore.** The seed's `.obsidian/workspace*` pattern (`gitignore:2`) also matches `workspaces.json`, so saved workspaces, which Advanced URI and QuickAdd switch between for the floor, stay untracked and a seeded layout is never committed. Whether that is intended is open.

**7.22 Egress.** Publish uploads selected notes to Obsidian's servers, Sync replicates the whole vault to a paid remote, Remotely Save syncs to a user remote, and Editing Toolbar's AI and Copilot send content to remote providers. Workers recorded egress as a concern separate from B3 and did not rank it.

**7.23 Current version for T5.** Settled for this record at desktop 1.13.7 (§1.1); `B-community-1` and `B-community-2` asked that T5 name the version it ran on.

**7.24 Core toggles that community rows depend on.** Several community results turn on a core-plugin setting: Longform's `sceneTemplate` applies only with core Templates (or Templater) enabled (`note-utils.ts:26-32`); Advanced URI's workspace switch needs core Workspaces enabled (`handlers.ts:163-168`); Calendar, Periodic Notes (through its fallback), Homepage's periodic kinds, TaskNotes' mini-calendar and timeblocking, Advanced URI's `daily=true` and Kanban's `link-date-to-daily-note` all route through core Daily notes and its folder (§7.5); Advanced URI's switch and QuickAdd's floor macros operate on the workspaces that core Workspaces saves (§7.21).

**7.25 The floor's pin.** The computed task for the supplementary run gave the floor commit as `a3d22d0a5ecf5592db40ef3a6a53b356553a4295`, which does not exist (the GitHub commits API answers 422; `git cat-file` fails in a fresh clone; both supplementary families and a verifier checked). The floor's `master` HEAD is `a3d22d0a9ecf5592db40ef3a6a53b356553a4295` (committed 2023-05-27). This record's short form `a3d22d0a` matches both, so no earlier cite changes; §2.5 names the full SHA.

**7.26 Version skew between the floor and the screened pins.** Every row pins the current upstream release, while the floor vendors older copies: Kanban 2.0.51 against 1.5.3, Longform 2.1.0 against 2.0.3, Zotero Integration 3.2.1 (screen C) against 2.3.9, Dataview 0.5.68 against 0.5.56, QuickAdd 2.30.0 against 1.0.2, Templater 2.25.1 against 1.16.0, Advanced URI 2.2.0 against 1.35.0, Focus Mode 1.12.0 against 1.11.4, Footnotes 0.2.0 against 0.1.2, Minimal Theme Settings 9.0.0 against 6.3.1, and in §3.5 Better Word Count 0.10.1 against 0.9.6, Hider 1.7.1 against 1.2.4 and Tag Wrangler 0.6.5 against 0.5.8. Pandoc Plugin is not skewed (0.4.1 in both; the `B-floor-config` desk listed it as skewed and its verifier corrected that), nor are Auto Note Mover, Typewriter Scroll and Contextual Typography. B1 and B5 for the floor's exact versions are therefore separate questions from these rows, and settings shapes moved: the floor's Better Word Count `data.json` uses 0.9.x keys that 0.10.1 ignores, and Hider's settings schema changed between 1.2.4 and 1.7.1 (§4.6). Floor-version licences are unrecorded for all 17; one spot check found Longform 2.0.3 (tag commit `71be087`) under the same FAFO License v0.2 as 2.1.0.

**7.27 One reading of a `package.json`-only licence.** Typewriter Scroll fails B5 because its only grant is an MIT string in a `package.json` that is not shipped, with no LICENSE file and no copyright notice. Tag Wrangler passes B5 although the monkey-around code it bundles rests on an ISC declaration in that repository's `package.json:8` alone (`pjeby/monkey-around@0884a10`; no LICENSE file). The `B-floor-vault` verifier asked that one reading decide both. Typewriter Scroll's successor, Typewriter Mode (`davisriedel/obsidian-typewriter-mode@3bed45b`, MIT), is outside the candidate set and was not screened.

**7.28 Contextual Typography's licence conflict.** `LICENSE.md` is GPL-3.0 and GitHub agrees, but `package.json:12` says MIT. B5 was read under the LICENSE file; if it is seeded, the GPL text and a Corresponding Source offer travel with it (§7.8). 1.13.7 now supplies the plugin's `el-<tag>` classes natively, but not its `data-tag-name`, `el-embed-*` or `el-lang-*` markers, on which the floor's Minimal theme scopes rules.

**7.29 Notices missing from the floor-vault bundles.** A seeded copy would have to add notices for `@popperjs/core` (Auto Note Mover); the Svelte 3.59.2 runtime and moment 2.29.4 (Better Word Count); azu/codemirror-typewriter-scrolling (Typewriter Scroll); and yaml, `@ophidian/core`, uneventful, monkey-around and to-use (Tag Wrangler). The floor's own vendored bundles embed third-party licence headers (the tslib notice in 7 bundles; further notice lines in Zotero Integration 31, Kanban 20, QuickAdd 14, Pandoc Plugin 13 and Dataview 10) with no LICENSE or NOTICE file beside any plugin, and the floor repository grants no licence, so a licence to vendor any floor copy has to come from each upstream at the matching tag (§7.8).

**7.30 Tag shape decides the reach of the tag-driven writers.** Literature notes' Zotero tags are `{tag, type}` mappings that Obsidian's tag parser drops (§4.2 Tags view, §4.6), so Auto Note Mover and Tag Wrangler reach literature/ only through body hashtags, and their machine-surface exposure is wiki/, whose claude-obsidian pages carry string-list tags (§7.19). Tag Wrangler's B3 fail rests on wiki/ and on the `B-core-1` gesture reading (§1.1, §7.1). Auto Note Mover's B3 hazard needs one live fact: whether 1.13.7 fires metadataCache `changed` for a file the CLI writes from outside Obsidian (§6.4). Resolved 2026-10-04 by the second ruling.

**7.31 Floor facts that contradict earlier rows.** `B-floor-config` found three earlier B2 credits to the floor that the floor's configuration does not bear out: §3.1 and §4.2 Word count credit the floor, but the floor turns core Word count off (`a3d22d0a .obsidian/core-plugins-migration.json:21`) and counts with Better Word Count; §3.1 Properties view credits the floor, but the floor predates Properties and has no such id; §3.1 Footnotes view credits the floor, but the floor uses the community plugin, and §4.1 Footnotes view itself notes the core plugin is newer than `a3d22d0a`. The footnote-insert pin that §4.1 Footnotes view and §7.14 cite as floor evidence (`command-palette.json:4`) names a command the vendored `obsidian-footnotes` 0.1.2 does not register, so it is dead (§8.2). Separately, §4.1 Files and Note composer describe a rename or move rewriting links in every linking note; the `B-floor-config` verifier found that the link update rewrites only links whose resolution or regenerated text changes (`app.js:1@byte 1792033`), so a move under shortest-format basename wikilinks rewrites nothing (§8.3). Recorded; the earlier rows are unchanged.

**7.32 Stale and per-machine values in the floor's `.obsidian/`.** Not seedable verbatim: the graph filter and a starred bookmark on a "1. Notes" folder that does not exist; an enabled CSS snippet with no file; dead command-palette pins (footnote insert, reading time) and dead hotkey ids (the footnote insert and a case variant of the Zotero export command); Pandoc Plugin's `outputFolder`, an absolute path on the author's macOS machine; `publish.json`'s Publish site identifier; a root-level `.vault-stats` listing paths from the author's personal vault; and the Zotero template's singular `cssclass` key, which 1.13.7 ignores. Joins §7.12.

**7.33 Hotkey collisions in the floor.** Templater's extracts sit on Mod+Z (undo) and Mod+T (core new tab, `app.js:1@byte 2851520`); QuickAdd's macros sit on Ctrl+N (core new note), Ctrl+S (core save, `@byte 2843182`) on Windows and Linux, and Ctrl+L, which probably collides with toggle-checklist (`@byte 3850880`; registered as lowercase, case handling unconfirmed).

**7.34 Link form in floor-shaped content.** Under `newLinkFormat` `shortest` (the default, unset in the floor), links Obsidian writes are basename-only and do not resolve as paths for a plain-Markdown reader; OKF §6.1 recommends bundle-absolute or relative links (`SPEC.md@ad30107:439-456`), so only `useMarkdownLinks` true with `newLinkFormat` `"relative"` yields standard-resolvable links. Five floor templates (city, event, institution, person, work) and the Templater research-note extract hard-code wikilinks whatever `useMarkdownLinks` says. Joins §7.6.

**7.35 Restricted mode is held per app, not per vault (2026-10-04 ◊).** On 1.13.7, restricted mode is the app-local localStorage key `enable-plugin-<appId>` under `~/.config/obsidian/Local Storage`, and community plugins count as enabled only when its value is `"true"`. `plugins:restrict` reported `on` while the key was absent. `app.plugins.setEnable(true)` set it to `"true"`, wrote nothing under `.obsidian/` and created no `community-plugins.json`; `plugins:restrict` then reported `off`. `setEnable(false)` writes `"false"` rather than deleting the key. The CLI's own help lists `plugins:restrict on|off` as a toggle. A seeded `.obsidian/` therefore cannot take a new vault out of restricted mode, as §1's screen facts state. Method: CLI `eval` and `plugins:restrict` against the scratch vault (`/tmp/lane23-live/obsidian/logs/step2-restricted.log`, `step4-restore.log`, `step0-cli-help.log`).

**7.36 minAppVersion does not gate a seeded plugin folder (2026-10-04 ◊).** In the 1.13.7 asar (sha256 `a52a7daf…`, as in §8), `minAppVersion` is compared only in the community-store install and update path, against `versions.json` and the app version the renderer receives over IPC (1.13.7). Neither plugin loading nor enabling reads it, so a folder seeded into `.obsidian/plugins/` cannot be refused for its `minAppVersion`; every B1 desk `minAppVersion` in §3 is at or below 1.13.3 in any case. The same host reports two versions: `obsidian version` gives 1.13.7 (installer 1.12.7), the renderer user agent says 1.12.7, and the IPC version is 1.13.7. Method: grep of the local uncompressed asar and CLI calls (`/tmp/lane23-live/obsidian/logs/step0-minappversion-gate.log`, `env.log`).

**7.37 What 1.13.7 writes when plugins are toggled (2026-10-04 ◊).** Joins §7.4, §7.16 and §7.21. `enablePluginAndSave` writes `.obsidian/community-plugins.json`, and `disablePluginAndSave` rewrites it as `[]` rather than deleting it. Every core-plugin enable or disable rewrites `core-plugins.json`. Enabling core Workspaces creates `workspaces.json`. Written into the config dir on first load with no seeded settings: Calendar's `data.json` (231 B, with `workspace.json` growing), Footnote Shortcut's `data.json` (638 B) and, when it loaded under the override, Novel word count's `data.json` (1,885 B); the other eight community rows run wrote none within 3 s. Once, after a screen-C plugin (Better Pandoc Reference List) was disabled, `community-plugins.json` was 0 bytes: it still held that plugin's id at 01:48:22, was empty at 01:48:25 and 01:48:30, and read `[]` again by about 01:48:44, before the next plugin was enabled. No logged action rewrote it, so the writer is unknown, and the observed empty span is at least 5 s; the leg had reported at least 12 s, and its verifier corrected that from the timestamps. Method: per-plugin vault-tree hashes before and after each toggle (`/tmp/lane23-live/obsidian/logs/plugin-*-tree-*.txt`, `step1-core.log`, `plugin-better-pandoc-reference-list.log`, `plugin-citation-suite-tree-0-before.txt`).

**7.38 This host's language list breaks one plugin (2026-10-04 ◊).** The WSL Obsidian process runs with `LANG=C.UTF-8`, so `navigator.languages` is `["en-US","c"]`, and an `Intl` constructor given the whole list throws a RangeError on the tag `c`. That is why Novel word count 5.0.0 fails to load here, which the leg recorded as a B1 fail (§4.5). ¤ The cause can now be stated: on Linux, Electron 39.8.3 drops only glib language names that are exactly `C`, so a `C.UTF-8` or `C.utf8` process locale, this host's WSL default and common in containers and minimal installs, leaves `c`. Bare `C`, an unset `LANG`, POSIX and language_COUNTRY.codeset locales without an @modifier give only accepted tags in the offline reproduction, and Windows and macOS take their tags from the OS language preferences. Novel word count B1 is therefore needs-live¤, with the failure recorded as conditional on the locale. Whether this host is representative of users' desktops is open (at the run; superseded ¤: the open part narrows to @modifier locales, §7.43, and to the unmeasured load under a valid list, §6.5 Not run). Method: CLI `eval` of the same `Intl` call with the host list and with `["en-US"]` alone (`/tmp/lane23-live/obsidian/logs/plugin-novel-word-count-diagnosis.log`); ¤ the Electron source and the offline reproduction in §7.42.

**7.39 Driving Obsidian through its CLI (2026-10-04 ◊).** `obsidian eval` does not await a Promise (a Promise result prints a blank line), and it sometimes prints a blank line with exit status 0 for an ordinary expression, so the leg stored results in window globals and read them back, re-running blank reads. Exceptions print as an error line. `dev:console` needs `dev:debug on`, a debugger attach; `dev:errors` captures uncaught errors and rejections, but Novel word count's load failure appeared only in the console. `plugins:enabled` lists the enabled core set. Method: the run's own command logs (`/tmp/lane23-live/obsidian/logs/step1-core.log`, `plugin-*.log`, `step0-cli-help.log`).

**7.40 Release asset hosts (2026-10-04 ◊).** Footnote Shortcut 0.2.0's release assets are served from `github.com/Comprehensive-Jason/obsidian-footnotes`; the catalog repository `michabrugger/obsidian-footnotes` redirects there. Every release-asset download in the run returned 200 (51, including screen C's). Method: release listing and downloads (`/tmp/lane23-live/obsidian/logs/step2-release-assets.log`, `step2-downloads.log`, `step2-download-hashes.txt`).

**7.41 First open of a research-vault scaffold, and the run's restore state (observed 2026-10-03 and 2026-10-04 ◊).** When 1.13.7 first opened the scaffolded scratch vault (2026-10-03 19:00), it wrote `app.json` and `appearance.json` as `{}`, `workspace.json`, and a `core-plugins.json` in object form holding all 31 registered ids. `publish` false, `sync` true and `webviewer` false are among them, and 21 ids are true. `git status` showed `.obsidian/` untracked, with `workspace.json` ignored. After the 2026-10-04 run, the vault's `.obsidian/` is byte-identical to that first-open state, restricted mode is on again, and no community plugin is installed (§6.5, Restore). Method: directory listing and hashes at step 0 and at restore, and the verifier's reported re-check at 02:02 (`/tmp/lane23-live/t5-first-open.txt`; `/tmp/lane23-live/obsidian/logs/step0.log`, `step4-restore.log`).

**7.42 Novel word count's load failure depends on the process locale (2026-10-04 check ¤).** A post-run check re-read the run's Novel word count leg against primary sources, and an adversarial verifier re-checked every deciding line and refuted none; where the two differ, the verifier's reading is the one recorded. The causal chain:

- (1) The plugin builds three `Intl.NumberFormat` objects at module top level from `navigator.languages` plus `en-US`, with no guard, so one invalid tag in the list aborts the module load (`isaaclyman/novel-word-count-obsidian` `logic/locale_format.ts:1-11` @`5bfba09`, MIT, `LICENSE:1-3`; the file's history is commit `24b8150`, 2023-12-30, then `24b77c5`, and its shape is unchanged since the first; the release bundle the run installed, `/tmp/lane23-live/obsidian/downloads/novel-word-count/main.js:670-680`, sha256 `65d5f85a…`, carries the same three calls).
- (2) The Obsidian binary on this host reports Electron 39.8.3 and Chrome 142.0.7444.265 (`strings` on `/opt/Obsidian/obsidian`, 2026-10-04). It is the 1.12.7 installer shell running the 1.13.7 asar (§6.5 Environment, §7.36), so a fresh 1.13.7 installer may ship another Electron; the verifier recorded that the glib filter in step 3 has been stable since 2020. In that Electron, `navigator.languages` is the app locale followed by the preferred languages (electron/electron @`v39.8.3` `shell/browser/api/electron_api_web_contents.cc:924-938`).
- (3) On Linux the preferred languages come from glib's `g_get_language_names()`, which reads only the environment; Electron drops the names that are exactly `C` and canonicalises the rest (`shell/common/language_util_linux.cc:21-31`, GetPreferredLanguages, @`v39.8.3`, paraphrased). Under `LANG=C.UTF-8` glib gives `C.UTF-8`, `C`, `C`, and `C.UTF-8` survives the filter as `c`.
- (4) This host is Ubuntu 24.04.5 LTS under WSL. `/etc/default/locale` sets `LANG=C.UTF-8`, `locale -a` lists only C, C.utf8 and POSIX, and the running Obsidian main and renderer processes have `LANG=C.UTF-8` in their environment (`/proc/<pid>/environ` of the main and renderer processes, read 2026-10-04). The run's diagnosis log shows `navigator.languages` `["en-US","c"]`, the RangeError in `eval`, and a load under the `["en-US"]` override (§4.5).

Offline reproduction (2026-10-04). A session-local Python script (`/tmp/nwc-check/langs.py`, not in the raw-log archive) called `g_get_language_names()` in the host's libglib-2.0 2.80, which Obsidian links (per `ldd`), dropped the names that are exactly `C`, canonicalised the rest with the system ICU 74.2 (`uloc_canonicalize`), and put `en-US` first; each list was then given to the plugin's Intl call in node 24.21.0 (ICU 78.3). `C.UTF-8` and `C.utf8` give `["en-US","c"]`, as observed; an unset `LANG` and bare `C` give `["en-US"]`, the override's list; `en_US`, `en_GB`, `he_IL` and `de_DE` with `.UTF-8` give lists node accepts (`en_US.UTF-8` gives `["en-US","en","en"]`); POSIX gives `posix`, which node accepts. Chromium bundles its own ICU, so the `C.UTF-8` to `c` step rests on the live log, not on this reproduction, and the other lists are inferred for Electron. node's RangeError message differs from the one Obsidian logged; the error class is the same. On Windows and macOS, Electron takes the list from the OS language preferences (GlobalizationPreferences.Languages; NSLocale preferredLanguages; `shell/common/language_util_win.cc`, `language_util_mac.mm` @`v39.8.3`), which are BCP 47 tags. An issue search of `isaaclyman/novel-word-count-obsidian` on 2026-10-04 found nothing for the exact phrase "language tag" or for RangeError; unquoted, language tag returned two unrelated issues (#133, #4).

The other 28 B1◊ rows carry no host-specific observation, and no cell changed. None of the 10 other community bundles run reads `navigator.languages` (grep of their downloaded `main.js`). Dataview (`main.js:8637`) and Calendar (`main.js:894`, `:3818`) read only `navigator.language`, the app locale. Better Word Count's only `Intl.NumberFormat` is Chart.js's lazy helper, which takes a caller-supplied locale (`main.js:10016-10030`). All 10 logs show the enable resolved true, the plugin loaded and no error captured; Calendar's one console line is a debug line about the Moment locale, driven by `navigator.language`. Contextual Typography's blank first readback is the CLI quirk in §7.39. The 18 core rows are Obsidian's own code and loaded on the same list with no captured error (`step1-core.log`). Screen C's evidence record already states the general caveat (`2026-10-03-substrate-components-design-obsidian-zotero-screen-evidence.md`, §7 item 27).

**7.43 Do @modifier locales break the load? (open; 2026-10-04 check ¤).** Linux sessions with a glibc @modifier locale, for example `sr_RS.UTF-8@latin`, `ca_ES.UTF-8@valencia` or `de_DE@euro`, produce tags such as `sr-RS@latin` in the glib and ICU 74.2 reproduction (§7.42), and node 24.21.0 rejects them in the plugin's Intl call. That would make Novel word count 5.0.0 fail to load on some Linux desktops with an ordinary locale. Chromium 142 bundles its own ICU, so this is inferred, not measured in Electron. Deciding check: relaunch Obsidian with such a `LANG` and enable the plugin.

**7.44 How is a locale-conditional load failure recorded against B1? (open; 2026-10-04 check ¤).** Does B1 judge a plugin only on an ordinary desktop locale, or also on the process locales research-vault's users actually run? The `C.UTF-8` default under WSL Ubuntu (this host) and in containers makes Novel word count 5.0.0 fail to load every time, and the plugin does not guard the call (§7.42).

**7.45 Which writes into literature/ does research-vault detect? (2026-10-04, third ruling.)** All three families surveyed it at `3780fb7` (§7.50). evidence-layer is commit-closing and runs from the vault pre-commit hook. It reports an added, deleted or renamed `literature/*.md`, a changed body (with the `managed-sha256` witness validated), and a changed capture-owned key, unless `generated` also changed to a machine-class writer. A nested directory is reported by the tree check, and changed claim blocks by claim-immutability (warn tier). Undetected: non-capture keys such as the machine-written `verified` and `failed-verification` lists; a restore of an earlier machine-rendered version, whose different machine-class `generated` counts as attested and whose witness matches its own body; and non-`.md` files placed flat in literature/. Whether Obsidian's frontmatter re-serialisation changes a capture value is not established. Verifiers: upheld by all three, with additions: a deletion is flagged whatever the attestation, and a bare new file is stamped `type: "literature"` by stamp-type, so evidence-layer, not okf-frontmatter, flags it (`research_vault/lints.py:275-336`, `:586-601`, `:618`, `:661-678`, `:698-725`, `:727-875`; `literature_notes.py:64-68`, `:108-132`; `structure.py:28-31`, `:184-219`; `stamp.py:126-133`; `events.py:10`, `:72`; `templates/git/pre-commit:29`).

**7.46 Which writes into log/, the review queue, `system/propagations/` and root `log.md` are detected? (2026-10-04, third ruling.)** The append-only lint covers `log/`, `inbox/review-queue.md`, `system/propagations/` and `projects/*/search-log.md`. It flags only a rewrite or deletion of committed bytes, and it is warn tier: verify files the finding into the review queue, but it is not in the commit-closing set. Appends pass. The pre-commit hook runs stamp-type before verify and stages what it stamps, so a bare new `.md` under log/ is stamped `type: "daily"` and passes okf-frontmatter. Root `log.md` is outside append-only, and okf-structure checks only its date headings and newest-first order. Verifier corrections (`B-core` and `B-top20` not upheld as stated): `okf.regenerate_log` treats every `log/*.md` as a day file and writes a heading per file stem into root `log.md`, so a non-date-named foreign log/ file is reported by okf-structure once the CLI next regenerates the log (capture, publish, scaffold), and the finding names `log.md`, not the file; a date-named foreign file is never reported. stamp-type walks the whole worktree, not the staged set, and git-adds every file it stamps, so it sweeps untracked bare `.md` files under inbox/, log/ and literature/ into the commit; it also runs inside capture and publish. `B-rest`'s verifier added that verify skips every `log.md` in okf-frontmatter and `check_reserved` falls back to the whole text on a parse error, so an unparseable or nested frontmatter block on root `log.md` is reported by nothing (a probe returned okf-structure matched) (`research_vault/lints.py:109-157`; `verify.py:621-660`, `:752-757`, `:796`; `structure.py:30`, `:117-134`; `okf.py:19-37`; `stamp.py:45-52`, `:93-157`; `capture.py:568`; `publish.py:428`; `templates/git/pre-commit:24-26`).

**7.47 Which writes into wiki/ are detected? (2026-10-04, third ruling.)** wiki/ has no content-drift check: the compile wrapper pins only the ledger's hash, and markers skip wiki/. Reported: a new `.md` that is untyped or unparseable (okf-frontmatter; wiki/ derives no folder type, so any non-empty type passes); nested `index.md` frontmatter and `log.md` shape (okf-structure); an unresolved wikilink (captured-set); a `[@key]` missing from the bibliography (citation-key, commit-closing); quote and disputed-claim breaks, through `file_outcomes`; and propagation residue of re-keyed keys. Not reported: body and frontmatter edits that keep a type and resolvable links, link-preserving renames, deletions of unlinked pages, and non-`.md` files such as `.canvas` and `.base`. Verifier correction (`B-core`, not upheld as stated): captured-set resolves wikilinks only against `.md` page names, aliases and citation keys, so an inserted wikilink or embed to a non-`.md` target is flagged (this detects Audio recorder's default-form embed and Unique note creator's dangling link), and deleting or renaming a linked page without updating its links is flagged. `B-top20` and `B-rest` verifiers upheld their surveys (`research_vault/structure.py:74-114`, `:136-182`; `captured.py:94-109`, `:133-191`; `verify.py:446-455`, `:745-751`; `compile.py:320`; `markers.py:73-76`; `lints.py:275-336`).

**7.48 Which writes into fulltext/ are detected? (2026-10-04, third ruling.)** The vault template gitignores fulltext/, and the hook verifies the index candidate, so the commit gate never sees it. A default worktree verify (the post-capture run, the publish gate, verify-citations), whose snapshot includes ignored files, reports only a new untyped or unparseable `.md` there. Edits, typed creates, deletions and non-`.md` files go unreported: the recompile check compares the ledger's hash with the hash the literature note records, not with the file's bytes, and lifecycle hashes cached text only when a Zotero attachment has already drifted, on the network leg. Verifiers: upheld, with `B-core`'s correction that the commit gate is blind to fulltext/ because it is gitignored (`research_vault/templates/vault/gitignore:5`; `templates/git/pre-commit:29`; `verify.py:752-757`, `:855`, `:873`; `__main__.py:805`; `publish.py:371-373`; `gitstate.py:247-277`; `captured.py:227-262`; `lifecycle.py:111-150`).

**7.49 Empty files, non-`.md` files and `system/bibliography.json`. (2026-10-04, third ruling.)** okf-frontmatter flags the missing `type` of an empty `.md` everywhere except: inbox/ fleeting notes; files named `index.md` or `log.md`, which verify skips and whose empty nested form passes the reserved-file check; log/, where the hook stamps the file first; and literature/, where stamp-type stamps `type: "literature"` and evidence-layer flags the unattested addition instead. The commit gate checks only what is staged, so an unstaged empty wiki/ file is not checked at commit. Non-`.md` files are checked nowhere: evidence-layer globs `literature/*.md`, verify enumerates `*.md` only, and the tree check reports only directories. `system/bibliography.json` is reported only when it is unparseable, through citation-key. Verifiers: `B-core` not upheld as stated (the literature/ stamp and staged-only corrections above); `B-rest` upheld (`research_vault/verify.py:723-741`, `:745-757`; `structure.py:161-163`, `:185-219`; `stamp.py:126-133`; `lints.py:586-601`; Obsidian 1.13.7 `app.js:1@byte 1776050`, `@byte 3153506`, `@byte 3341238`).

**7.50 Code pins of the third normalisation (2026-10-04).** `research_vault/` and `hooks/` are identical at `3780fb7` and at the worktree HEAD `b06e212` (`git diff --stat 3780fb7 HEAD -- research_vault hooks` is empty). The core evidence is `app.js` from the 1.13.7 asar: the release `.asar.gz` sha256 is `69253e39…`, as §1.1 records, and `app.js` is 3,876,459 bytes with sha256 `8efbf581…`, re-extracted in a session-local directory and byte-identical to the earlier extraction. `B-rest` read the local 1.13.7 asar under `~/.config/obsidian/` (sha256 `a52a7daf…`, as §8 records). Plugin sources were re-read at each row's pin. Verifier: upheld.

**7.51 What `app.json` `userIgnoreFilters` confines (2026-10-04, third ruling).** It confines Backlinks' unlinked-mention Link, Properties view's vault-wide rename and delete, and Bases row edits, because each skips ignored files. It does not confine writes into the active note (Outgoing links, Outline, Footnotes view, Templates, Unique note creator, Audio recorder, Slash commands), the File properties pane, popovers, or the link updater. Joins §7.7. Verifier: upheld (`app.js:1@byte 1758536`, `@byte 3025992`, `@byte 1780900-1781500`, `@byte 2512194`, `@byte 3426900`).

**7.52 Obsidian's link update after `renameFile` (2026-10-04, third ruling).** In 1.13.7 `renameFile` wraps `vault.rename` in `runAsyncLinkUpdate`. With `alwaysUpdateLinks` true the update runs at once; otherwise it runs after the person confirms an Update links modal, which under the third ruling does not remove the path. Joins §7.6 and §8.3. Verifier: upheld; paraphrased, since the app is UNLICENSED (`app.js:1@byte 1777261`, `@byte 1777501`, `@byte 1779273`).

**7.53 Git hooks, CI and the PreToolUse guard in a scaffolded vault (2026-10-04, third ruling).** Only `.git/hooks/pre-commit` is installed. Under git 2.43.0 (`git help githooks`, read 2026-10-04) a merge commit runs `pre-merge-commit`, which runs pre-commit only when that hook is enabled and is not installed here, and a fast-forward runs no hook; verify's base defaults to HEAD, so pulled content is never verified locally. The isomorphic-git bundled in Obsidian Git 2.41.1 exposes only `onPrePush` and `onPostCheckout`, so mobile commits run no pre-commit hook. The CI replay workflow is opt-in (`with_ci` defaults to false). The PreToolUse guard denies only Edit, Write and NotebookEdit on roster paths, and only where the research-vault plugin loads; Bash and non-Claude agents are outside it (§7.10). Verifiers: upheld (`research_vault/scaffold.py:105`, `:225`; `templates/ci/verify.yml`; `gitstate.py:318-321`; `hooks/hooks.json:6`; `hooks/pretooluse_guard.py:11-23`).

**7.54 The flat parser and Kanban and Excalidraw frontmatter (2026-10-04, third ruling).** A frontmatter block whose first line is blank fails `research_vault.frontmatter.parse` with a bad-line error, measured on Kanban's basic board frontmatter, a saved board and Excalidraw's drawing frontmatter (rerun 2026-10-04); block lists indented by two spaces parse. Verifier correction: Kanban's view switch rewrites the board through Obsidian's `processFrontMatter`, which drops the blank line, so a switched board parses (§4.3 Kanban). Excalidraw makes no `processFrontMatter` call (`research_vault/frontmatter.py:150-182`; obsidian-kanban `src/parsers/common.ts:24-27`, `src/parsers/formats/list.ts:442-451` @`8501981`; obsidian-excalidraw-plugin `src/constants/constants.ts:469-479` @`128a9ef`).

**7.55 Disabling versus confining after the third ruling (2026-10-04).** The third ruling withdrew the invocation readings and the recoverable reasoning. It neither withdrew nor restated the second ruling's sentence that disabling a component outright is not a confining setting, or the second pass's reading that a setting whose only effect is to stop the plugin's function disables it (§2A, Second ruling). All three families kept both. Local REST API (both servers off) and Auto Note Mover (`"folder_tag_pattern": []`) fail on them and would pass if a loaded-but-idle setting counted. Verifier: upheld; the second of the two is a pass reading, not an operator ruling.

**7.56 The vault guide's detection claim (2026-10-04, third ruling).** Vault `AGENTS.md:28` (`research_vault/templates/vault/AGENTS.md:28`, identical at `3780fb7` and HEAD) says machine-surface hand or tool edits are regenerated away or raise a finding. The surveys (§7.45-§7.49) find no finding for wiki/ content edits that keep a typed, parseable block and resolvable links, root `log.md` edits that keep its shape or give it an unparseable block, fulltext/ bodies, appends to log/ files or `inbox/review-queue.md`, or non-`.md` files in literature/, wiki/ or fulltext/. Verifier: upheld.

**7.57 research-vault detection gaps the third normalisation surfaced (2026-10-04).** Recorded as facts for the issue tracker; this pass opened no issue.

- (1) The vault pre-commit hook runs stamp-type over the whole worktree and git-adds every stamped path, so untracked bare `.md` files under inbox/, log/ and literature/ are swept into the commit, and a foreign log/ file is stamped `type: "daily"` and passes verify (`research_vault/templates/git/pre-commit:24-26`; `stamp.py:45-52`, `:131`, `:150`; `structure.py:30`).
- (2) `okf.regenerate_log` treats every `log/*.md` as a day file without checking that the name is a date. A non-date foreign file breaks root `log.md`'s shape at the next regeneration, and okf-structure names `log.md` rather than the cause; a date-named foreign file is never detected (`okf.py:22-32`; `structure.py:117-134`).
- (3) evidence-layer's `_write_attested` accepts any diff in which `generated` changed to a machine-class writer, so restoring an earlier capture-rendered literature note (File recovery, Publish, Sync, an old checkout) counts as attested, and its `managed-sha256` matches its own body; only lifecycle's network leg could notice the older `zotero-item-version` (`lints.py:661-678`).
- (4) The machine-written `verified` and `failed-verification` lists are outside the capture fields, so an edit or deletion of either goes unflagged, and `record_pass` only appends, so a deleted `verified` history is never restored (`lints.py:618`; `literature_notes.py:64-68`; `events.py:100-141`; `verify.py:548-576`).
- (5) fulltext/ is on the guard roster but gitignored by the vault template, so the commit gate never checks it, and offline nothing checks its content (`hooks/pretooluse_guard.py:11`; `templates/vault/gitignore:5`; `lifecycle.py:129-138`).
- (6) captured-set resolves wikilinks only against `.md` pages, so any wikilink or embed to an attachment (image, audio, PDF) in a wiki/ page is reported as not captured, a latent false positive; whether the compile tool emits attachment embeds was not checked (`captured.py:94-108`, `:173-188`).
- (7) verify skips okf-frontmatter for every `index.md` and `log.md` at any depth, and `check_reserved` passes an empty nested one, so empty reserved-name files in machine folders go undetected (`verify.py:755`; `structure.py:161-163`).
- (8) Root `log.md` is on the roster, but only its heading shape is checked, so content edits go undetected until the next `regenerate_log` silently overwrites them (`structure.py:117-134`; `okf.py:19-37`).
- (9) Root `log.md` frontmatter is never checked: okf-frontmatter skips it and `check_reserved` falls back to the whole text on a parse error, so OKF §11 rule 1 is unenforced there (`verify.py:752-757`; `structure.py:119-123`).
- (10) Non-`.md` files placed in literature/, wiki/ or fulltext/ are reported by no check (`lints.py:586-601`; `verify.py:745-757`; `structure.py:185-219`).
- (11) wiki/ has no content-drift check, and vault `AGENTS.md:28` claims more detection than the code provides (§7.47, §7.56).

## 8. The floor vault's configuration

This section follows §7 so that no earlier section is renumbered. Family `B-floor-config` read every file under the floor's `.obsidian/` at `a3d22d0a` (full SHA in §2.5), screened nothing, and had a verifier. It is evidence for tuning the seeded `core-plugins.json`, and the `app.json` keys several rows depend on (§7.6), not a verdict: it records what the floor sets and what each setting would do in a research-vault vault. Values are the verified ones; **†** marks a cell the verifier corrected, and the desk value follows each table. The floor is unlicensed, so its settings are paraphrased; key names and values are cited as facts. Floor cites are at `a3d22d0a` and relative to the floor's `.obsidian/` unless they begin with `meta/` or say root. `app.js` cites are byte offsets (`app.js:1@byte N`), as in §4.2, into the same 3,876,459-byte `app.js`, read from the uncompressed `obsidian-1.13.7.asar` (sha256 `a52a7daf1e2460bae03de80f2816604bd16a56cd374fbe5ce8d1a9ef5604059d`, byte-identical to the local `~/.config/obsidian/obsidian-1.13.7.asar`); §1.1's hash `69253e39…` belongs to the release asset `obsidian-1.13.7.asar.gz` (both hashed with `sha256sum` under `/tmp/obs` on 2026-10-03).

### 8.1 How 1.13.7 loads the floor's core-plugin files

The floor uses the 2023 layout: `core-plugins.json` is an array of 17 enabled ids (`:2-18`), and `core-plugins-migration.json` maps 28 ids to booleans (`:2-29`). 1.13.7 still reads the pair. It starts from the migration map; for each registered id in its list of 26 legacy ids (`app.js:1@byte 2922510`) it sets the state to whether the id is in the array; any registered id missing from both files falls back to `defaultOn`, and a config save is requested (`@byte 2924109-2924854`). `saveConfig` then writes the object form over `core-plugins.json` (`@byte 2924854`). `starred` is in the legacy list but registered by no plugin, so the loop skips it, yet the key survives in the object saved back. `canvas` and `bookmarks` are not in the legacy list, so their state comes from the migration map alone. Nothing deletes `core-plugins-migration.json`; the only other code that names it is Sync's file classifier (`@byte 3631492`).

Effective state on 1.13.7: 19 on (the 15 array ids that are in the legacy list, `canvas` and `bookmarks` from the migration map, and `properties` and `bases` by `defaultOn`) and 12 off (daily-notes, slash-command, markdown-importer, zk-prefixer, random-note, word-count, slides, audio-recorder, publish, sync, footnotes, webviewer). 21 ids are `defaultOn`; the floor differs from 1.13.7's defaults on four: daily-notes, word-count and sync off, workspaces on. What 1.13.7 actually writes on first opening such a vault is a live leg (§6.4). The research-vault seed has no `.obsidian/` today (`research_vault/templates/vault` at `3780fb7`).

Desk values the verifier corrected (†):

- Files: the desk said a move rewrites links in every linking note, with no confirmation. The verifier found the rewrite limited to links whose resolution or regenerated text changes (`app.js:1@byte 1792033`); the confirmation modal is skipped only when there is a change to make.
- Canvas: the desk said new `.canvas` files go to the `app.json` new-file location. The verifier found Canvas registers its own file-parent creator, which reads `canvas.json`.
- Command palette: the desk said only the reading-time pin is stale; the footnote pin is dead too.

The verifier also tightened two cells without the flag: Templates' B4 (the inbox deviation, and the hard-coded wikilinks) and Note composer (a merge still asks for confirmation; only the link-update prompt is skipped).

Desk values the verifier corrected (†):

- Kanban: the desk said 1.5.3 has no vault event hooks, and missed the block-id write and the new-board placement.
- Footnotes: the desk said the floor's two commands are insert footnote and insert auto-numbered footnote, both bound to one chord, with insert footnote pinned.
- Longform: the desk said index frontmatter is rewritten when scenes change in its pane; the triggers are vault events and startup too.
- Auto Note Mover: the desk called it the floor's only background writer and said each move rewrites links in every linking note.
- Advanced URI: the desk said it writes only for write, append or daily parameters.
- QuickAdd: the desk said each macro copies the URI and reopens the note, and named only the Ctrl+N collision.
- Pandoc Plugin: the desk called the pandoc binary path a macOS path.

The verifier also added, without the flag: Templater's Mod+T collision and hard-coded wikilink; Tag Wrangler's tag-page placement and missing `type`; the Zotero Integration dead hotkey id and image copying outside the vault API; and Better Word Count's root-level `.vault-stats`.

### 8.5 Other configuration and the floor's write chain

- **Write chain.** `newFileLocation` `"current"` places Templater extracts, Note composer extracts, quick-switcher notes and Kanban card notes beside the active note; Auto Note Mover, in Automatic mode, then files them by tag with `renameFile`, but only when the destination folder exists (`plugins/auto-note-mover/main.js:1822-1829`); `alwaysUpdateLinks` true then updates links with no prompt, though a move leaves basename wikilinks untouched (`app.js:1@byte 1792033`). † The desk said the last step rewrites every linking note. Longform's index-frontmatter writes are a second background writer.
- **Templates and OKF.** The floor's ten core templates carry no YAML, only tag lines and Dataview inline fields, so a note made from them breaks rule 1 outside inbox/. The Zotero template carries YAML whose `type` is the Zotero item type.
- **`appearance.json`:** font size 17, Moonstone light, the Minimal CSS theme, and three enabled snippets, one of which has no file in `snippets/`.
- **`snippets/`:** `callouts.css` colours callouts by Zotero highlight colour, scoped on a research-note CSS class (see the `cssclass` finding in §8.4); `hidecitations.css` hides Pandoc citation spans in reading view.
- **`themes/Minimal`:** version 6.3.3, minAppVersion 1.1.0.
- **`hotkeys.json`:** nine bindings; collisions in §7.33, dead ids in §8.4 (Footnotes, Zotero Integration).
- **`workspace.json`:** the live layout, whose active leaf shows the floor's readme; the last-open file list; ribbon configuration. † The desk said the active file is the Zotero template; that holds only in the legacy extension-less `workspace` file, from pre-1.0 Obsidian. `workspaces.json` holds the three named layouts (§8.2). The seed's `gitignore:2` pattern matches all three files.
- **`vault-stats.json`:** Better Word Count's daily statistics, 2023-05-23 to 2023-05-27. **Root `.vault-stats`** (outside `.obsidian/`, found by the verifier): an older statistics format with 64 days from 2022-01-02 to 2022-05-23, carrying file paths from the author's personal vault.
- **`community-plugins.json:2-18`** lists exactly the 17 folders under `plugins/`, one to one, all enabled; no plugin folder is disabled and no listed id lacks a folder.
- **Stale references** are collected in §7.32.

## 9. Classification (2026-10-04)

The class is this record's sorting of each row under the operator's rule, "include anything that aids a workflow and does no harm" (§2A "Inclusive rule and five workflows"); it is not a verdict, and the spec carries the verdict. Each class is the verifier's. Workflows are numbered as in §2A: (1) research, (2) analysis and reports, (3) personal knowledge management, (4) long-form writing, (5) daily logs. Settings are `file key=value`; for a community plugin, a bare key is in its `data.json`. Condition names what the class waits on, a hinge that would move it, or a cost the spec must weigh; "B1 needs-live" means the load has not been run yet (§6.5). Rows run SEED, RECOMMEND, CONDITIONAL, EXCLUDE, and within each class in §3 order.

Counts: 39 SEED (23 core, 7 top-20, 9 seed, search-find and floor), 4 RECOMMEND, 2 CONDITIONAL, 26 EXCLUDE (8 core, 9 top-20, 9 others); 71 rows.

Facts across rows that the spec needs once, from the verifiers:

- No seeded `.obsidian/` ships until the dotfile packaging blocker is fixed: the package-data glob does not match dot-paths, and the scaffold's dotless renames cover files, not directories (§7.11).
- While #194 is open, a vendored licence or notice file with a `.md` extension under `.obsidian/plugins/` is held to OKF rules 1-2 and closes the commit gate (§7.11). Ship such files without the extension; otherwise the SEED rows that vendor one become CONDITIONAL on #194.
- #194's body says its nested-YAML parser finding is filed on its own, but no such issue exists (`gh` search, 2026-10-04). Kanban's and TaskNotes' conditions rest on that clause, and the blank-first-line rejection that hits Kanban boards and Excalidraw drawings (§7.54) is not nested YAML; one parser issue covering OKF-valid YAML generally would carry both.
- With `useMarkdownLinks=true`, every link the person autocompletes takes the markdown form, not only the links plugins write (Audio recorder row).
